# Token Log

One line per landed slice, appended in the same commit that lands the slice - part of
the existing paperwork motion, not a separate ritual (docs/TokenBudget-Plan.md S3, AC9).

Figures come from `tools/Get-TokenUsage.ps1 -GroupBy Session` for the session that
produced the slice, on this machine's transcripts only. Costs are ESTIMATES at
first-party list rates (the tool prints its rate table); the model column is the model
that implemented the slice. Slices sharing one session share one line, labelled so.

The committed baseline these compare against is `.agents/token-baseline.json`
(August 2026; regenerate on or after 2026-09-01 so the full month is in it).

Format: `date  slice  model  reqs N  mean-ctx NK  est $X.XX`

The search now issues ONE REQUEST PER FIELD concurrently - startswith(deviceName,'v'), startswith(userPrincipalName,'v'), serialNumber%20eq%20'v', same $top/$select - merged by device id with first occurrence winning, ordered by device name, Truncated if ANY page carried @odata.nextLink, SearchedCount the distinct merged count, blank term still one unfiltered request; encoding narrowed so only the typed value is escaped (single quote doubled first) while OData syntax stays literal, exposed through three internal static URL builders pinned by exact-string tests; T7 is per field - any one non-2xx fails the whole search naming that field's request, never a partial or silent-empty result; and a defensive client-side re-check drops rows that do not actually match, counting them in FilterIgnoredCount which the page states as a warning, so an endpoint that ignores a filter cannot render an arbitrary page as matches. - 2026-09-11 CloudPasswordReset S0 derivation rebuilt for three-source agreement (owner go "go", scope: the two tool files and their tests; the S0 re-run is NOT covered - "I approved ONE"). The superseded design matched on the whole UPN, which is why the owner's own account missed: cloud key "michael.coelho", on-prem sAMAccountName "mcoelho", on-prem UPN "michael.coelho@<domain>". Source 3 now compares the UPN LOCAL PART to a local part (userPrincipalName=<key>@*, proxyAddresses=smtp:<key>@*, sAMAccountName=<key>), source 1 is employeeId OR'd against employeeID/employeeNumber/extensionAttribute1, source 2 is the display name with a trailing -CLD/_CLD stripped. employeeType is NOT a fourth id attribute (it holds CWK, a worker class, a category not a person); manager and otherMails stay struck. The rule that carries the safety property is an asymmetry: an IDENTIFIER arm matching more than one user is DISCARDED as non-evidence and the other sources still decide, while a PERSON arm matching more than one REFUSES as AmbiguousWithinSource, because two people sharing a display name are two real candidate people. Cost falls from 2 x domains queries per candidate key to ONE OR'd query per domain, agreement computed in memory. Tests rebuilt 40 -> 74; suite 214/0; PSScriptAnalyzer no Errors. Three mutation probes, all BITE: invert the id-arm discard, revert source 3 to a whole-UPN arm, drop Get-ObjectPropertyValue from Export-ModuleMember. That last one is a new test class worth keeping: Invoke-PlanOrAction never runs its scriptblock in -PlanOnly, so a script calling an UNEXPORTED module helper parses clean, plans clean and dies on the first real account - the module had exactly that bug. Cost paid twice to the same trap: "return ,$array" reaches the caller as ONE pipeline object, so @(f) and "f | Should" re-nest it; that produced the false 0% in 8feb993 and 12 test failures here, and is now documented in the module, a source-text tripwire and a calling-convention docblock. Also self-inflicted: my throwaway probe script used -like against needles containing [ ], let Set-StrictMode leak into Pester, and aborted mid-probe with the module mutated on disk. One session, opus, continued after a compaction.

The one approved S0 run returned 46.5% owner coverage (80 of 172 in-scope privileged cloud-only accounts) and all 84 misses returned ZERO directory rows, not wrong people - the accounts simply have no on-prem object findable by any of the three sources. Split by naming era: "Last, First" 68/79, "First Last" 12/66, single-token 0/19, and every one of the 31 underscore-UPN accounts missed. Two mechanical fixes (underscore-to-dot separator normalization, flipped display-name order) would have reached ~84%, not 100%. employeeId is populated on 0 of 172 cloud accounts, so source 1 contributed nothing and the three-source design ran as two; the owner refused to populate it ("we're not going to change several hundred cld accounts"). Deleted the whole derivation apparatus from the plan (~380 lines), collapsed three audit fields to one required destinationAddress, rewrote AC3/AC4/AC5, added the D4 gate (does the reveal permission still fence anything, now that the operator can reach the password by addressing the mail to themselves - recommendation: keep it, stop calling it a security boundary), and compressed the state.md block from ~208 lines to ~60. The judgement call worth recording: I refused to let the plan quietly keep its old safety language. The superseded claim - that the operator never learns the password, so resetting a Global Admin is a nuisance rather than a takeover - is FALSE under the new design, so three passages now state in plain words that prevention was traded for detection (section access + audit + admin alert), that the owner made the trade knowingly, and that the phone-call process it replaces has neither property. The fifth question - can a broken page still satisfy the ten tripwires - produced msr-1 (MEDIUM, admitted after confirming the mechanism line by line, not on the reviewer word): the tripwires assert CloseUserReport() precedes the refetch, and RefreshBatchUsers does exactly that, bumping the generation BEFORE its await and never nulling batchUsers, so the old rows keep rendering for the whole Exchange call with a Report button gated on neither loadingBatchUsers nor actionInProgress (Migration.razor:737-739, unlike the refresh button beside it at :750 and every action button at :706-727). A report opened in that window captures the post-bump generation and writes itself over rows since replaced. ExecuteUserAction has the same shape and a narrower window; the other five sites are safe because they null batchUsers or move expandedBatch. No code authorized - the fix is a follow-up slice needing a go. Records only: finding doc, index row, plan Implementation review section, state.md. Dispatch scratch is gitignored per the 2026-08-07 index note. One session, opus-5, continued after a compaction; token usage not measured.
- 2026-09-17 msr-1 fix, migration report vs in-flight row reload (docs/MigrationStaleReport-Plan.md): the owner said "fix it" without naming an option, so the choice was mine. Took the structural route rather than the two-site patch the reviewer suggested: one private ReplaceBatchUsers helper owns every replacement of the rendered rows and closes the report itself, and all seven assignment sites hand it the awaited fetch directly, which is what puts the close after the await. Rejected gating the Report button on loadingBatchUsers - one attribute, but it blocks a read-only diagnostic and leaves the invariant unenforced. The useful lesson is about the tripwires, not the code: the original ten asserted the close precedes the refetch, which the defective code did, so they were satisfied by the bug. The replacement assertion is per occurrence and negative - no path assigns batchUsers outside the helper - and it is the only one an eighth reload path cannot slip past. Guard proof: three mutations, all bite, restore byte-identical; mutation 1 is literally the pre-fix code and the original ten passed it. 52 MigrationStatusPageTests green, full suite green, format clean. Module 1.8.1 -> 1.8.2, no base app bump. One session, opus-5, continued through two compactions; token usage not measured.

Lesson: review the plan, not just the diff; a design flaw that the tests are also blind to cannot be caught downstream. All seven findings verified against the file before acceptance rather than taken on the reviewer word; all seven were real. Commit B fixes the live instance the review found: SearchUser cleared loadingBatchUsers after each await while its finally cleared only isSearching, so a thrown Exchange call left it stuck for the life of the circuit. Two of the seven failed on first run against CORRECT code: the scanners matched the words "await" and "finally" inside the new explanatory comments, so a StripLineComments pass is now mandatory in any source scanner here. - 2026-09-18 Service Health load feedback (docs/ServiceHealthLoadFeedback-Plan.md, owner-reported queue item 7, steps 1-5 in one slice): the complaint reads as "add a spinner" and state.md correctly warned it is not - the page already had three correct spinners. Root cause found by reading, not guessing: the page prerenders (Program.cs:374 AddInteractiveServerRenderMode, no prerender:false anywhere in the app), prerendering emits no HTML until OnInitializedAsync reaches quiescence, and OnInitializedAsync ended with await LoadAsync, so the Delinea read, the Graph token and both Graph collections all completed before the browser received its first byte - the spinners were unreachable by construction. The operator-visible half is worse and is what produced the repeated clicks: enhanced navigation (App.razor subscribes to blazor:enhancedload) leaves the PREVIOUS page fully rendered and interactive during the fetch, so there is no visual change at all. Owner chose shape B, the BlockedSenders.razor:166-181 precedent, over disabling prerender. Also resolved a question state.md had flagged unverified: the authorization round trip is NOT part of the problem - GroupAuthorizationHandler.HandleRequirementAsync is synchronous over in-memory state and returns Task.CompletedTask, no I/O - which is why the auth check stays in OnInitializedAsync in both the precedent and here. Step 3 matters more than it looks: Blazor does not auto-render after OnAfterRenderAsync, so without StateHasChanged in LoadAsync's finally the deferred load leaves a permanent spinner, which is worse than the bug. Step 4 fetches the two Graph collections under Task.WhenAll instead of serially; no existing test asserted the second call is skipped when the first fails, so the failure-path change is compatible. Five tripwires, every one stripping comments first - the migration work's lesson applied pre-emptively, and it mattered, because this change's own comments name OnAfterRenderAsync, LoadAsync and StateHasChanged. Guard proof: steps 1, 2 and 3 mutated back independently, each 1 failed / 34 passed against its named tripwire, all three restores byte-identical by SHA256 with the mtime touched. Verified: build Release 0 errors and the same 23 pre-existing warnings, dotnet test 2510 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped, format clean, git diff --check clean; no PowerShell touched. Module 1.3.1 -> 1.3.2, no base app bump. Noted not fixed: prerender plus the interactive circuit each ran OnInitializedAsync, so every Service Health view was writing TWO audit entries; this change incidentally drops it to one, and whether other pages do the same is unexamined. Not done: the plan's manual acceptance checklist (no bUnit harness) and the implementation codereview. Also observed: the origin gitea remote is unreachable, SEC_E_CERT_EXPIRED. One session, opus-5; token usage not measured.

- 2026-09-18 click-gating slice 0A, the audit scanner as a repo tool (docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md): the owner approved slice 0 + slice 1 + tier 1 only, so the plan status is now Approved in part with tiers 2-4 explicitly out of scope. This commit lands the instrument the audit was produced with, which until now existed only in a temp file and was therefore not durable memory. tools/Get-ClickGateAudit.ps1 plus 14 Pester tests. Three classification rules are the whole value and each was wrong in an earlier revision: (1) in-flight flags are detected STRUCTURALLY, never by name - this app uses actingDeviceId, isCsvProcessing, detailLoading, so a name list undercounts - and the test that a set-once latch is not a flag pins the revision that made every page look broken; (2) the flag must be lowered in a FINALLY, which is the only thing separating a gate from a nullable result field, and without it Migration read as 15 flags instead of its documented 8; (3) comments are BLANKED, not deleted, so reported line numbers still point at real code - deleting shifted every line, which is worse than reporting none because it sends the reader to innocent code. Calibrated against Migration as a known-good control: 8 named flags, IsBusy, the four documented exemptions at 223/442/784/836 and the three tab anchors at 30/33/36, all reproduced exactly. Full run reproduces the plan's published totals: 33 pages, 273 buttons, 108 ungated, 12 data-only, 89 partial, 209 gap, 7 non-button targets, 3 predicates. Disclosed limits kept honest in the header: text analysis not semantic analysis, no bUnit harness exists, findings are candidates for a human read, and the known false positives (expandedBatch, bulkOutcome, opResult, resolution, targetProtection, previewResult, detail) are named in the plan. Verified: 14/14 Pester, PSScriptAnalyzer clean on both new files with zero new findings repo-wide, ASCII lint passed. No C# touched, so no build or dotnet test needed for this commit. One session, opus-5 with ultracode; token usage not measured.

- 2026-09-18 click-gating reconnaissance, Revision 1 of docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md: before building the shared harness, ran an 11-page read-only workflow over slice 1 + tier 1 - one deep read per page, two adversarial refutation lenses each (is the control-flow reading factually right / would the proposed gate trap the operator), then a synthesis designing the registry. 34 agents, 0 errors, ~4.2M subagent tokens, 69 minutes. It paid for itself several times over: it falsified four load-bearing claims in the design the owner had just approved, and every one would have shipped as a defect. (1) There is NO ErrorBoundary anywhere in the app (zero matches across Components/, Services/, Program.cs), so a throw from a handler tears the circuit down rather than leaving a live page with a stuck flag - which means the planned "lower every flag in a finally" assertion does not close the risk it was written for, and the real page-deadener is a call with no timeout or cancellation token (BlockedSenderService.UnblockSenderAsync:48 is the live example). (2) The plan prescribed a handler guard for non-button targets; on any DOM-synced control that CORRUPTS DATA, because the backing field is unchanged so the render diff emits no correction and the browser keeps the operator's action - on NamedLocations it writes the OLD country set to Graph permanently, since CountryCodePicker latches _initialized on first parameter push. Five refutations found this independently on five pages. Three refusal mechanisms are needed where the plan had one. (3) The scanner cannot see the controls that matter: its "7 non-button targets app-wide" is an @onclick-only count, while @onchange, InputFile, @onkeydown, @bind and child Disabled= are invisible - across these 11 pages alone the real number is about 35. (4) A page-wide predicate is provably wrong on SelfServiceGroups (two mutually exclusive views, the only "unsound" verdict) and ConferenceRooms (background OnJobChanged callback). Also caught: the obvious slice-1 fix is WRONG - wrapping BlockedSenders ConfirmUnblock 278-414 in try/finally moves the lowering after the LoadBlockedSenders call at 413, at which point a busy guard in that callee silently no-ops the post-unblock refresh and the table keeps showing the sender just unblocked. The remedy is a catch converting the throw into a PermissionResult.Fail, mirroring 351-356. Estimate for the approved scope revised 8-11 -> 20-29 sessions. Two live defects found in passing, neither caused nor fixed by this work and both needing an owner go: IntuneDevices.razor:403 WipeNameConfirmed is the ONLY enforcement of the typed device-name confirmation on a factory wipe (ExecuteActionAsync never re-reads wipeConfirmName), and BlockedSenderService.UnblockSenderAsync has no CancellationToken and no timeout. Lesson worth keeping: the recon was read-only and cost one workflow, and it caught a design flaw that all eleven planned assertions would have passed - the same lesson as the 2026-09-17 plan review, one level up. Records only this commit; ClickGateSource.cs is written and compiles but is held uncommitted because the registry it serves changed shape. Verification: git diff --check clean; no code touched. One session, opus-5 with ultracode.

- 2026-09-18 click-gating slice 0B, the shared tripwire suite and its registry (docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md, owner approved option A): three new files replacing what would otherwise have been ~200 near-identical per-page tests. ClickGateSource.cs is the source reader generalised from MigrationStatusPageTests' per-page helpers, with two lessons baked in rather than left to each caller - comments are BLANKED not deleted so offsets and line numbers stay valid against the real file (EnclosingMethodName and every assertion message work from offsets, so a stripped-but-shifted source reports defects at innocent lines), and tag walking is quote-aware because a naive <button.*?> stops at the > inside a lambda arrow and reads a truncated tag. ClickGateRegistry.cs holds one entry per converted page plus a declared NotYetConverted list covering all 32 other pages with a reason each; every field in the record was forced by a specific page during the recon and the forcing page is named on the member so none gets dropped later as unused. ClickGateTests.cs has 14 assertions. Seeded with Migration ONLY - the single page whose gating is already known-good - so the suite is green today and each page joins as it is converted; registering the partial IntuneDevices/RiskyUsers predicates now would have shipped a red suite. The design win is EveryPageIsRegisteredOrDeclaredUnconverted: a new module's page is in neither list, so it fails and names itself, which makes the 2026-09-17 rule self-enforcing instead of dependent on whoever reviews the next module. Guard proof, four mutations, each failing exactly its named assertion at 1 failed / 15 passed with restores byte-identical by SHA256 and mtime touched: ungating a gated button, folding pendingActionLabel into IsBusy, deleting the tab-anchor handler guard, and removing a page from both registry lists. Honest limits written into the class doc rather than discovered later: no bUnit harness exists so nothing renders, text containment proves an identifier is present and never that it is load-bearing (disabled="@(IsBusy && false)" passes everything), the finally assertion does NOT close the risk the original plan wrote it for because there is no ErrorBoundary in the app and a throw kills the circuit regardless, and EveryNonButtonClickTargetIsRegistered only finds @onclick - @onchange, InputFile, @onkeydown, @bind and child Disabled= are invisible and must be hand-enumerated. Verified: build Release 0 errors and the same 23 pre-existing warnings, dotnet test 2526 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped (up exactly 16), format --verify-no-changes exit 0, git diff --check exit 0, ASCII lint passed. No PowerShell touched this commit. Tests only, so no version bump. One session, opus-5 with ultracode.
- 2026-09-18 click-gating slice 1a, the BlockedSenders stuck flag (docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md): ConfirmUnblock raised isLoading at the top and lowered it only by straight-line statements on three normal-exit paths, while the authorization round trip (GetAuthenticationStateAsync + AuthorizeAsync), the protected-principal gate and OperationTrace.BeginOperation all sat outside every try. The recon's warning was the whole value of this slice: the OBVIOUS fix is wrong. Wrapping the method in try/finally moves the isLoading clear after the LoadBlockedSenders call at the end, and once that callee consults a page-level busy predicate - which is the entire point of the sweep this slice unblocks - the confirming refresh silently no-ops and the operator is told the unblock succeeded while the sender stays listed. The fix is therefore a catch around the preflight that converts the throw into a PermissionResult.Fail and returns, mirroring the write catch already in the method, plus an audited UnblockSender_Denied row so a preflight that could not complete is still accountable. Fail closed: the catch returns rather than falling through, so an unreadable authorization answer can never become an unauthorized Exchange mutation. Six tripwires, all over comment-stripped source via ClickGateSource - which mattered here, because the new explanatory comments contain the literal strings "isLoading = false" and "LoadBlockedSenders" that two of the assertions search for. Guard proof, three mutations: hoisting the first preflight await above the try failed exactly PreflightAwaitsAreInsideTheTry + OnlyTaskYieldAwaitsBeforeTheTry (2 failed / 4 passed); deleting the isLoading clear from the preflight catch failed exactly PreflightCatchFailsClosed (1 failed / 5 passed); and the naive try/finally on the tail failed exactly LowersIsLoadingBeforeTheRefresh + HasNoFinally (2 failed / 4 passed). All restores byte-identical by SHA256 with the mtime touched. Worth recording: the first planned mutation - dropping the `return;` from the preflight catch - could not be run, because the compiler rejects it with CS0165 on the unassigned gate/scope locals. The fail-closed return is enforced by the type system, not only by the tripwire, which is a stronger guarantee than the test alone. Also hit and worth remembering: Serena's replace_content rewrites the whole file with CRLF on Windows, which fails `dotnet format` in this LF-normalised repo (.gitattributes forces eol=lf for .cs/.razor/.md); three files had to be converted back before the gate passed. Verified: build Release 0 errors and the same 23 pre-existing warnings, dotnet test 2532 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped (up exactly 6), format --verify-no-changes exit 0, git diff --check exit 0, ASCII lint passed. No PowerShell touched. Module 1.4.0 -> 1.4.1, no base app bump. Not done: the page is NOT converted to a gated predicate - this slice only removes the prerequisite hazard - and the manual acceptance check needs a dev deploy because no bUnit harness exists. One session, opus-5 with ultracode.
- 2026-09-18 click-gating slice 1b, the MessageTrace stuck flag (docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md): ToggleDetail raised detailLoading at 976 and lowered it at 987 and 996, both nested inside `if (token == detailRequestToken)`, with no finally anywhere in the method. The escape is the Audit.LogLookupAction at 992, which sits inside the catch and therefore outside every try - a throw there left the method with the flag raised and every detail button on the page dead for the life of the circuit. Fixed as a token-guarded finally. The token guard inside the finally is load-bearing and is NOT the obvious simplification: a bare clear would let a superseded fetch, completing after a newer click has taken the token, lower the NEWER request's flag and re-enable the button while that fetch is still in flight. Four tripwires; the negative one is per occurrence per the msr-1 lesson - it locates the finally with BlockAfter, then asserts every regex match of `detailLoading = false;` in the comment-stripped body falls inside that block, with no ordering assertion, so a method that both lowers correctly and leaks elsewhere still fails. Guard proof, four mutations, all real test failures rather than build failures: (A) the exact pre-fix shape, lowering back inside both token branches with the finally deleted, failed LowersDetailLoadingOnlyInsideTheFinally + FinallyKeepsTheTokenGuard at 2 failed / 8 passed; (B) the token guard dropped from the finally failed FinallyKeepsTheTokenGuard at 1/9; (C) deleting `detailRequestToken++` from RunTrace failed RunTrace_StillRescuesDetailLoadingAndSupersedesTheToken at 1/9; (D) moving the raise after the first await failed RaisesDetailLoadingBeforeItsFirstAwait at 1/9. All restores byte-identical by SHA256 with the mtime touched. Incidental confirmation that comment stripping works: the new explanatory comment inside the finally contains the literal `if (token == detailRequestToken)` and mutation B still bit. Verified: build Release 0 errors and the same 23 pre-existing warnings, dotnet test 2536 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped (up exactly 4), format --verify-no-changes exit 0, git diff --check exit 0, ASCII lint passed. No PowerShell touched. Module 1.4.1 -> 1.4.2, no base app bump. **Slice 1 is now complete and tier 1 is unblocked.** Noticed and deliberately not fixed, as a separate finding for a separate commit: the unwrapped Audit.LogLookupAction calls inside catch blocks are the page idiom here (six sites across ToggleDetail, RunTrace, AnalyzeHeadersAsync, the CSV download and the email job). The new finally means a throw at 992 now lowers the flag on its way out, but the throw still escapes the handler and, with no ErrorBoundary in this app, takes the circuit. AnalyzeHeadersAsync has the same shape with its flag already safe. Implemented by a coding subagent under the owner's 2026-09-18 weekend ruling; orchestrator owned the records, the commit and the push.
- 2026-09-18 click-gating tier 1 page 1 of 9, DhcpAuthorization (docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md, owner-approved option A): the first page through the shared harness, and the point of the exercise was to turn the per-page cost from an estimate into a measurement. Result: 362 -> 411 lines, ONE registry entry, ZERO new test methods - the 14 shared assertions instantiate for the new page and the full suite goes 2536 -> 2550, which is exactly +14. That is the economic claim of slice 0B holding up on first contact. Predicate is `isLoading || isOperating || isDownloadingCsv`; the third flag did not exist, because DownloadCsvAsync owned none, and its raise sits BELOW the `if (servers.Count == 0) return;` guard per Revision 1's warning that a raise above an early return leaks the flag true forever and, in a page-wide predicate, kills the whole page. The three `@bind` inputs are DOM-synced so they take the disabled attribute - the only safe mechanism per falsification 2 - and are deliberately NOT registered as NonButtonTargets, because that list is keyed to what ClickGateSource can locate and an `<input>` is in neither Tags("a") nor NonButtonClickTargets(), so registering one would FAIL an assertion rather than document anything. Five snapshot-at-entry changes, each closing a named post-await live read: the two `operationResult.Success` reads after an email await (273 and 348) were the NRE the ungated dismiss could cause, which then audited and emailed a SUCCEEDED forest-level AD write as failed and skipped the confirming refresh. M3a and M3b were caught by the COMPILER (CS0103), stronger than a test but reported as not-the-test-biting. **M3c is the real finding and is recorded as a gap, not smoothed over: a size-preserving revert of the operationResult snapshot passes every assertion in the suite** - only the incidental line-count fingerprint moved. That measures Revision 1's falsification 6, which said the design has no slot for the snapshot obligation; the only thing standing behind those two changes today is a code comment and registry prose. Two more unasserted fields recorded: PrerequisiteBeforeExemptionHolds (added to the record, no assertion - the agent could not touch ClickGateTests.cs under its scope) and RaiseMustFollowEarlyReturn (listed in Revision 1's design table, no field, no assertion). One obstacle hit and cleared correctly: renaming the empty-list guard broke a PRE-EXISTING tripwire pinning `if (servers.Count == 0)` shared by four sibling CSV pages, and the agent reordered its own code rather than touching the load-bearing guard. - 2026-09-18 click-gating harness, the three missing assertions (docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md): slice 0B shipped the registry without PostAwaitLiveReads, which Revision 1's design table listed as required and falsification 6 forced. Page 1's guard proof MEASURED the cost - mutation M3c, a size-preserving revert of the operationResult snapshot, passed all 40 assertions and was caught only incidentally by the line-count fingerprint - so the most important fix on that page stood on a code comment. This slice closes it before page 2, because falsification 6 names six pages. 14 -> 19 assertions, 40 -> 46 instantiated, full suite 2550 -> 2556, exactly 3 new theories x 2 registered pages. M3c now fails with the handler, the field and every offending line number. The field shape was derived from the page rather than invented, and needed a discriminator because there are genuinely two shapes: PublishedFromLocal, where the local is produced BY the await and then published (operationResult = result), which has no entry-time capture to check; and CapturedAtEntry, where the local IS the capture and must sit above the first await (newDnsName/newIpAddress/ticketNumber). The load-bearing subtlety is the definition of "read": WholeWord(id) NOT followed by an assignment, because `ticketNumber = "";` on the success path is a correct clear while `ticketNumber` passed to an audit call is the defect - without that distinction the assertion fails against correct code. PrerequisiteBeforeExemptionHolds is now asserted in BOTH directions and tied to machinery by parsing the field name out of the exemption's own registered snippet, so the note and the obligation cannot drift apart; direction A re-runs the real snapshot check rather than restating it. RaiseMustFollowEarlyReturn was implemented rather than documented-as-skipped, and its probe P4 was the ONLY failure out of 46, so nothing else in the suite sees that page-killing defect. Honest limits written into the comments rather than discovered later: all three are text containment, so none can prove the snapshot local is the value actually handed to the service call, and the early-return rule is textual ordering within one method body, not control flow. Guard proof, 8 probes, all size-preserving at 411 lines and compiling, all restored byte-identical with mtime touched: M3c 2F/44P, P2 (catch audits the live field) 1F/45P, P3 (capture hard-coded) 1F/45P, P4 1F/45P, P5a and P5b (registry edits in each direction) 1F/45P each, and P6a/P6b confirming ExtractBlock is not in this slice's dependency path - an unbalanced brace inside a string literal leaves the suite 46/46 clean. **Found and flagged before page 2: Migration cannot take a PostAwaitLiveReads entry as written.** It uses PublishedFromLocal correctly, but at 1495-1497 and 1727-1729 it does a deliberate null-safe save/restore across a refresh that itself nulls the field, which the read-after-first-await rule would flag as a false positive. Registering it needs a per-entry allow-list or a narrower dereference-only rule; the idiom may recur. Also noted: DhcpAuthorization line 83's ConditionThatKeepsItTrue is assertable but still prose, needing a Snapshot-less variant the page does not currently justify. Verified: build Release 0 errors / 23 warnings, 2556 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped, format exit 0, git diff --check exit 0, ASCII lint passed, DhcpAuthorization.razor byte-identical to HEAD. Tests only, no version bump. No reviewer round dispatched: the 2026-08-31 decision makes those CRITICAL-only with an owner go, and the coder-side guard proof closes this.
- 2026-09-18 click-gating tier 1 page 2 of 9, NamedLocations (docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md): 501 -> 538 lines, one registry entry, zero new test methods, suite 2556 -> 2573 (+17, exactly the 17 shared per-page theories instantiating for a third page). Predicate `isLoading || isOperating || isDownloadingCsv`, page-wide - and that was tested rather than assumed: the list and form panels render side by side and share every flag, because both write handlers end by calling LoadLocations, which replaces the collection the list renders AND invalidates editingLocation, a reference into the old list. isDownloadingCsv is new; its raise sits below the empty-list early return and is registered in RaiseMustFollowEarlyReturn. **CountryCodePicker needed no contract change and reaches nothing outside tier 1** - Revision 1's conditional did not fire, because the component already declares `[Parameter] public bool Disabled` and already applies it to all four interactive elements, and a repo-wide grep finds exactly one instantiation. The fix was one word. The latching hazard is real and is closed by the parameter, not by a handler guard, exactly as falsification 2 requires. Eleven PostAwaitLiveReads obligations, including falsification 6's named defect: EditLocation and ShowCreateForm blank formTicketNumber, so the audit row and admin email recorded a blank or wrong ticket. Two more found in passing that are worse: operationResult was read after the email await in both SaveLocation and DeleteLocation, so the exempt dismiss could null it and NRE the handler into its own catch, auditing and emailing a SUCCESSFUL Conditional Access write as failed - and on the delete path, reporting a location that really is gone as still present. Also `editingLocation` decides Create vs Update and supplies the target id, and CancelForm (previously ungated entirely) nulls it. Line 191's formTicketNumber clause is an IntuneDevices-403-shaped case: DeleteLocation does not re-check the ticket, so that disabled-expression clause is the SOLE enforcement of the ticket requirement on a deletion - now registered, so a mechanical rewrite fails. Guard proof, 7 mutations, all size-preserving at 538 lines, all restored byte-identical, no compiler catches: M1 ungate 1F/62P, M2 drop a preserved clause 1F/62P, M3 revert the operationResult snapshot 2F/61P (failing BOTH the new snapshot assertion and the exemption-prerequisite one - the hole c4fc09d was written to close, biting on its first new page), M4 revert a ticket snapshot 1F/62P, M5 the page-killing raise-above-early-return 1F/62P, M7 drop a flag from the predicate 1F/62P. **M6 is the measured gap and the page-1 M3c analogue: stripping `Disabled="@IsBusy"` from the CountryCodePicker leaves the suite 0 failed / 63 passed.** Nothing in the harness can see a child-component Disabled= parameter or any of the seven DOM-synced controls on this page; EveryNonButtonClickTargetIsRegistered only finds @onclick, and registering an `<input>` would fail an assertion rather than protect it. So those eight attributes rest on registry prose and review. On this page that is the single largest residual risk, because silently deleting one line reinstates the permanent-desync-writes-the-old-country-set-to-Graph defect with a green suite. Its own slice, not this one. Also found, not fixed: page 1's registry comment now carries a stale scanner claim, caused by page 1's own snapshot fix - `operationResult = result;` reads to the scanner as a raise with no finally, so it is now reported as a stuck flag. - 2026-09-18 click-gating harness, the DOM-synced blind spot (docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md): page 2's mutation M6 measured it - stripping `Disabled="@IsBusy"` from the CountryCodePicker left the suite 0 failed / 63 passed, on a control whose latching behaviour means a refused click permanently desyncs it and the next save writes the OLD country set to Graph. 19 -> 22 assertions, 63 -> 72 instantiated, full suite 2573 -> 2582 (+9, exactly 3 new theories x 3 converted pages). M6 now fails TWO assertions by name. 27 DOM-synced controls registered across the three converted pages, 5 recorded ungated with written reasons. The valuable half is completeness, asserted in BOTH directions: an input/select/textarea/Disabled-bearing child component on disk that is in neither list fails and names itself, and a registered line the enumeration no longer finds also fails - so a broken enumerator cannot silently pass, which is the cheapest-broken-implementation answer for that assertion. The enumerator is built on the quote-aware `Tags()`; ExtractBlock is deliberately not used anywhere in this slice and the comment says why (Migration's placeholder at 324 is an interpolated string full of braces). Child components are found by harvesting PascalCase tags and filtering on a disabled attribute, which is also what keeps C# generics out of the result; the honest limit - a child component with no disabled attribute at all is invisible, because nothing distinguishes it from a generic - is recorded, and the backward direction closes it from the other side once registered. Guard proof, 4 probes, all line-count-preserving so the fingerprint could not mask them: M6 2F/70P, M6b (narrow a gate from the predicate to one flag) 1F/71P, P2 (add an unregistered ungated input) 1F/71P, P3 (give a recorded-ungated control a gate) 1F/71P. **Three findings, all reported not fixed.** (A) Twelve of Migration's sixteen DOM-synced controls carry `disabled="@isLoading"` or `@isCreating`, not `@IsBusy` - its BUTTONS name the predicate, its form controls do not, which is the pre-ruling shape from docs/MigrationButtonGating-Plan.md. The assertion was therefore written to require a busy signal the page REGISTERS (predicate or member flag) rather than the predicate itself, because the stricter rule would have failed a converted page; that is a decision about Migration.razor, not about the test. (B) **A live gating hole on the converted precedent page:** Migration:390's search box and :821's staged-ticket box are `@onkeydown` paths whose handlers call SearchUser() and ConfirmPendingAction() with no busy guard, while the Search and Confirm buttons beside them ARE gated on IsBusy - so Enter reaches the operation the button refuses. ConfirmPendingAction is single-flight only by accident, nulling its callback before the await, which is re-entrancy protection and not a busy gate. A disabled input fires no keydown, so one attribute each closes both. (C) No assertion covers `@onkeydown` anywhere; the Enter-key path is the next uncovered click-equivalent, and the now-falsified "nothing detects a missed non-button control" comment was corrected rather than left standing. Verified: build Release 0 errors / 23 warnings, 2582 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped, format exit 0, git diff --check exit 0, ASCII lint passed, no .razor modified. Tests only, no version bump.
- 2026-09-18 Migration Enter-key bypass (found by the DOM-synced harness slice 017adb8, fixed as its own finding): the converted precedent page had two live gating holes that its seven bespoke tripwires and its codex review both missed, because nothing in this repo could see a keyboard path. Migration:390's search box and :821's staged-ticket box carried NO disabled attribute at all and routed @onkeydown to HandleSearchKeyDown -> SearchUser() and HandleConfirmKeyDown -> ConfirmPendingAction(), neither of which has a busy guard, while the Search button at :400 and Confirm at :825 beside them ARE gated on IsBusy. So the button greyed out and Enter still fired the operation - a direct violation of the owner's 2026-09-17 rule that a control is clickable only when its click will definitively execute. The staged-ticket one reaches a destructive action and avoided double-execution only BY ACCIDENT: ConfirmPendingAction nulls pendingActionCallback via CancelPendingAction before its await, which is re-entrancy protection, not a busy gate, and would not stop a destructive action starting during an unrelated refresh. Fixed with one attribute each - a disabled input fires no keydown, so both paths close without touching the handlers, and the handlers correctly keep no guard: the attribute is unconditional on IsBusy and re-evaluated every render, so there is no state where either input renders enabled while IsBusy is true. Ordering was checked rather than assumed: ExecuteBatchAction sets actionInProgress BEFORE its first await (:1456), so IsBusy is true at the first render after the confirm. The residual Enter-in-the-network-gap window is the same race every disabled="@IsBusy" button in this sweep already accepts, not a new one. :821 takes IsBusy alone and deliberately NOT `IsBusy || pendingActionLabel != null`, because pendingActionLabel is a registered ExcludedField and folding it in would disable the ticket box at the only moment it is rendered - the same trap the codex plan review caught before any code was written in September. Both attributes were appended to the existing attribute line so the file stays at 2025 lines and every registered line number is undisturbed. Guard proof, 3 mutations, all 1F/71P: M1 and M2 drop each attribute independently, and M3 - added by the agent unprompted, and the sharpest of the three - keeps both attributes but leaves :390 in UngatedDomSyncedControls, proving the registry list MOVE was mandatory rather than optional bookkeeping. Verified: build Release 0 errors / 23 warnings, ClickGate 72/72, MigrationStatusPage 60/60 (the seven tripwires expand to 60 cases), full suite 2582 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped with NO delta - the fix adds no test, it satisfies an existing registry assertion, which is the harness working as designed. Module 1.9.0 -> 1.9.1, no base app bump. Also corrected in this commit: a comment in ClickGateTests.cs that used these two entries as its worked example of an unjudgeable recorded-ungated reason. Rather than delete it, it now says the example is gone and the limitation rests on its own argument - a recorded-ungated entry is a promise to come back, and the only thing enforcing it is a reader. **Process hazard worth remembering: the Edit tool silently drops a trailing space at the end of a replacement string**, which produced two malformed registry lines mid-run; caught in the diff and repaired with WriteAllText. Still open, not fixed: @onkeydown is uncovered by any assertion anywhere, so this class is closed on Migration but not detected elsewhere; and Migration's other twelve form controls remain on single flags rather than the predicate, which is an unmade page-level decision.
- 2026-09-18 click-gating harness, keyboard paths (docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md): the last demonstrated blind spot, opened by 2eb8c15 - Migration's two Enter-key bypasses were invisible to every assertion because nothing matched @onkeydown. 22 -> 27 assertions (4 theories x 3 pages + 1 fact), 72 -> 85 instantiated, suite 2582 -> 2595 (+13 exactly). **The slice is PREVENTIVE and says so in its class remarks rather than letting a reader assume it caught something:** the census found no uncovered keyboard path on any converted page - Migration's two were closed by 2eb8c15, the other two pages have none, and no component instantiated on a converted page carries a keyboard handler (the three shared autocompletes that do are not used there). It will bite soon though: four of the seven remaining tier-1 pages already carry an @onkeydown search box (IntuneDevices:57, GroupManagement:38, M365GroupManagement:59, SelfServiceGroups:133). Two design points worth keeping. First, the **parse sentinel**: because two of three pages have zero keyboard handlers, a completeness assertion is vacuous there, and a silently broken scanner looks identical to a page with nothing to find - so an @onkey* attribute in a shape the regex cannot read is itself a failure, which removes "narrow the regex until the sweep is empty" as a way to pass. Second, the **GatedTwinButtonLine** check makes 2eb8c15's defect an invariant: where a keyboard path has a gated button twin, that twin must still exist, still reach the same operation and still name a busy predicate. Also enforced: a DisabledAttribute refusal only counts on input/select/textarea/button, because the attribute is inert on an anchor or div and keydown still fires - and the element name is read off the file, so mis-registering the tag fails the locate step rather than satisfying the check. **The agent found its own fixture was decoration and fixed it before running the probe, which is the report I most wanted:** its first version placed both stray `>` characters AFTER the keyboard attribute, so a non-quote-aware walker still mapped the handler correctly and P10 would have passed against a broken scanner. Moving one `>` ahead of the handler and adding a TagText containment assertion made it bite in both directions. That was the single thing in the slice whose absence could not have been detected from a green run. Guard proof, 11 probes, all bit, all hashes restored: P1 (strip the real gate) 2F/83P, P2 forward direction 1F/84P, P3 phantom entry 3F/82P, P4 mis-declared harmless 1F/84P, P5 mis-declared HandlerGuard 1F/84P, P6 wrong element type 2F/83P, P7 strip the twin button's gate 2F/83P, P8 unregistered handler on a zero-handler page 1F/84P, P9 and P10 scanner regressions that hit the FIXTURE ONLY while all three pages stayed green - which is the fixture earning its place - and P11 regex narrowed to nothing 4F/81P including the sentinel. P3 was broader than predicted and the reason is inherent rather than a weakness: the registry key is (line, event), so a mis-keyed entry trips both directions at once; only a markup-side change or an entry deletion isolates the forward direction. Verified: build Release 0 errors / 23 warnings (a full --no-incremental build was needed to reproduce 23; an incremental run reported 22 because the web project was skipped, not a change), 2595 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped, format exit 0, git diff --check exit 0, ASCII lint passed, no .razor modified. Tests only, no version bump. Still uncovered and now written into the comment rather than left implicit: @onchange and @onsubmit have no enumerator, and a keyboard handler inside a child component is invisible; the DOM-synced sweep reaches an @onchange control only when it happens to be an input/select/textarea, which is most but not a rule.
- 2026-09-18 click-gating tier 1 page 3 of 9, MailboxPermissions (docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md): 657 -> 730 lines, one registry entry, zero new test methods, ClickGate 85 -> 109 and suite 2595 -> 2619, exactly +24 = the per-page theories instantiating for a fourth page. Predicate `IsBusy => isLoading`, one flag, page-wide - tested rather than assumed: one view, and every write handler replaces part of the state the others render. **The sharpest fix is a live defect found during conversion, not one the audit predicted.** `ConfirmOnPrem` now opens `if (IsBusy || !onPremConfirmPending) return;` and the SECOND clause is the one that matters: SetTab and CancelOnPrem both clear `onPremConfirmPending`, so before this a Continue click that left the browser before the confirmation panel unrendered still executed - and because ExecuteOnPrem read the tab at its own entry, **a tab flip followed by a stale Continue ran a REMOVE against a staged ADD, on the destructive on-prem path.** Falsification 6's named defect was also real and worse than described: `tabIndex` was read after the awaits at the write itself (426), the Add-only self-grant check (363), the audit action string (434), the AutoMapping argument (443) and the notification direction (455), so a tab click landing mid-write **revoked the permission the operator asked to grant** and mislabelled the audit row and both emails on the way past. Twelve snapshot obligations across three handlers close it. Ten DOM-synced controls, not the six Revision 1 predicted - the four extra are the two ticket inputs and BOTH `RecipientAutocomplete` instances, which declare a `Disabled` parameter the page was passing nothing to. Both write handlers raised isLoading then awaited outside their try (SubmitSingle through re-authorization, ticket validation AND the protected-principal check), failing `NoRealAwaitSitsBetweenARaiseAndItsProtectingTry`; the try now opens below Task.Yield in both, with a finally, justified per handler - neither ends by calling a refresh helper, so the slice-1a hazard does not arise, and all eight scattered `isLoading = false;` lines were traced individually before deletion rather than swept (the only order-sensitive one, 422, sits after `onPremConfirmPending = true;` and the relative order is preserved under the finally). **Exactly-one-guard resolved by naming the method, not the pair:** ConfirmOnPrem carries the guard because it is what the click reaches and what the gated button belongs to; ExecuteOnPrem must never carry one, because it raises isLoading as its own first statement (so a guard there is a re-entrancy guard on itself) and because it is redundant today only while ConfirmOnPrem raises no flag first - the moment anyone "improves" that, the guard turns the whole on-prem write into a silent no-op with no banner, no audit row and no email. **M6 and M7 together locate the enforcement boundary exactly and the claim is trimmed to match:** M7 planted the BARE `if (IsBusy) return;` in ExecuteOnPrem and ForbiddenGuardSitesCarryNoGuard failed it; M6 planted the COMPOUND shape this page actually uses and the suite stayed 109/109. So the not-both half is enforced against the bare form and evaded by a compound one, and the must-have half has no assertion at all - both written into the entry rather than claimed closed. Guard proof, 7 probes, all size-preserving, all SHA-verified, no compiler catches: M1 1F/108P, M2 1F/108P, M3 1F/108P, M4 2F/107P (the page-2 CountryCodePicker lesson reproduced - stripping a child component's Disabled makes it invisible to the scan, so the backward direction fires too), M5 1F/108P, M6 0F/109P as predicted, M7 1F/108P. **Correction to the brief, and it matters for page 6:** this page instantiates `RecipientAutocomplete`, NOT `ADIdentityAutocomplete` - a different component with the identical ungated-suggestion-row defect. ADIdentityAutocomplete is 9 sites across 4 pages; RecipientAutocomplete is 8 sites across 4, reaching CalendarPermissions (page 4) and ConferenceRooms (page 8) inside tier 1 plus OutOfOffice outside it. Page 6 therefore inherits TWO contract changes. Recorded so it is not rediscovered. The subtlest point, now in the entry's remarks: on this page the snapshots matter more than the gate, because `Disabled` stops typing and closes the component's own Enter path, but a dropdown already open when the gate closes keeps clickable rows - and the blur-on-disable that would hide it is browser-dependent and no safety argument rests on it. The entry captures are what stop a late suggestion click retargeting the write, the audit row and both emails. `autoMapping` is fixed in code and deliberately NOT registered: `ReadOf` cannot tell a named-argument label from a field read, and the alternative was making it the 9th positional bool argument of an audit call - bending readable code to satisfy a text matcher. Named as unenforced; the remedy belongs in the assertion. Also found: **Get-ClickGateAudit.ps1 now misreports this page** as an 11-of-11 gap with `Predicate: -`, because its detector requires an expression-bodied bool naming at least TWO flags and this is the first converted page with one. Not a page defect, no test consumes it, and the entry says explicitly not to fix the page to satisfy it. Verified: build Release 0 errors / 23 warnings, 2619 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped, format exit 0, git diff --check exit 0, ASCII lint passed. Module 1.1.0 -> 1.2.0, no base app bump. Six pages remain.
- 2026-09-18 click-gating harness, three batched assertion fixes (docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md): all three were specified by the page-3 conversion after it MEASURED them, and all three serve page 4, which is the structural twin. 27 -> 29 assertions + 1 fixture, ClickGate 109 -> 114, suite 2619 -> 2624 (+5 = 4 theory cases across the converted pages + the new fixture fact). Tests only, no version bump. (1) **ForbiddenGuardSitesCarryNoGuard widened - the measured one, and the before/after pair is the proof.** It matched the bare literal `if (IsBusy) return;` and nothing else, so the compound guard page 3 actually uses was invisible: probe M3a planted that shape in ExecuteOnPrem against the UNMODIFIED harness and got 0 failed / 109 passed, reproducing the hole exactly; M3b ran the identical mutation after the fix and got 2 failed / 112. M4 replanted the bare shape as a regression control - still caught. The replacement is a shared BusyGuards matcher: an early return whose condition names a predicate or flag the page registers, brace-free by construction so ExtractBlock is nowhere in its path, reading two bracket levels and skipping depth 3 rather than half-matching. The honest limits are named in the comment rather than discovered later: the inverse `if (!isLoading) return;` is caught and mislabelled (safe direction), a report-then-return refusal is NOT caught, nor one behind a helper or a mid-body flag read - it proves absence of the guard SHAPES, never absence of refusal. (2) **ExactlyOneGuardOf added** - the field Revision 1's table listed and slice 0B shipped without. Three checks, and the pair of probes shows why all three are needed: M1 MOVES the guard from ConfirmOnPrem into ExecuteOnPrem and fails naming BOTH methods in one message, while M2 merely DELETES it and fails only the new assertion, with the forbidden-site assertion staying green - so the negative half alone would pass a page with no guard anywhere. The registered `Guard` is the if-condition only, not the whole statement, so page indentation stays out of the registry, and that it actually returns is checked against the file by the same matcher rather than taken from the string. (3) **ReadOf now excludes a named-argument label**, closing the page-3 trap where `autoMapping:` in an audit call was indistinguishable from a live field read. Proved in three directions because one would not do: M5a registers the obligation against the unfixed matcher and fails at the label (line 488); M5b passes after the fix; M6 reverts the capture to a genuine live read at line 472 - argument-start position with no colon, the sharpest possible discrimination, same position class as the label, opposite verdict - and fails. That third direction is what refuses the cheap answer of "stop matching autoMapping anywhere". The exclusion fires only when the identifier is at argument-start AND a colon follows immediately, which is what keeps a ternary, a case label, a goto label and an interpolated-string format specifier out of it; the spaced spelling `f(live : b)` is deliberately still caught, because that asks for a human. **The negative assertion's honest bad answer - break the matcher so it finds nothing - is closed two ways rather than left hanging:** a new page-independent fixture exercises the matcher against four shapes that must be found and three that must not, and fix 1 reuses the same matcher in the POSITIVE direction, so a matcher that stops finding things fails there too. No converted page failed under the widening, and the reason is recorded rather than assumed: none of the three refresh helpers has an early return at all, and ExecuteOnPrem/ReauthorizeAsync return only on non-busy report-then-return conditions. Verified: build Release 0 errors / 23 warnings, 2624 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped, format exit 0, git diff --check exit 0, ASCII lint passed, no .razor modified. The agent also scanned both files for the 2eb8c15 Edit-tool signature - a cross-line string join with no space on either side, which git diff --check cannot see because it is a DROPPED space - and found none.
- 2026-09-18 click-gating tier 1 page 4 of 9, CalendarPermissions (docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md): the structural twin of page 3, sequenced fourth by Revision 1 so the decisions are inherited rather than re-litigated - and the value of the report was in the five places where they could NOT be. 647 -> 679 lines, one registry entry, zero new test methods, ClickGate 114 -> 139, suite 2624 -> 2649 (+25). **Lead finding: `ExecuteOnPrem` writes a self-consistent lie into the audit trail.** It computes the audit action string (OnPrem_SetCalendar vs OnPrem_RemoveCalendar) and the recorded access level from `tabIndex` at entry, above Task.Yield, then re-reads `tabIndex` at the write three statements later and a third time for the notification direction. A tab flip in that window makes **the audit row and both emails say Set while the code performs a Remove**, on the destructive on-premises path. Not a stale value a reader could later spot as odd: a permanent, internally coherent record of a change that never happened, next to one that did and was never recorded. The two halves sit close enough to read as obviously paired, which is why it survived. The staleness half of the same defect - SetTab and CancelOnPrem both clear onPremConfirmPending, so a Continue that left the browser before the panel unrendered still executed against a flipped tab, running a REMOVE against a staged SET - was closed by the same `if (IsBusy || !onPremConfirmPending) return;` guard page 3 uses. **Five divergences from page 3, each called out rather than smoothed:** (1) `DownloadSampleCsv` is GATED here, not exempt - page 3's exemption rests on the handler serving a constant and reading no page field, and this one reads `bulkIsSet` twice, so copying the exemption with softened wording would have been precisely the inferred-exemption-that-looks-like-a-decision the registry exists to stop; three exempt controls, not four. (2) Eight DOM-synced controls, not ten - one `<select>` over eleven CalendarAccessRight values replaces page 3's three checkboxes, and `<option>` is outside FormElementTags so the options need no entries. (3) `onPremAccessRight` is captured at entry AND re-read live at the write, a genuine extra obligation page 3 lacks. (4) Six `tabIndex` read sites in SubmitSingle, not five, including one with no page-3 analogue: `tabIndex == 0 ? accessRight.ToString() : ""` decides whether the audit row records an access level AT ALL. (5) The lead finding above. **One deviation from the written spec, resolved and recorded:** SubmitSingle could not have its try extended upward the way page 3's was - CS0136, because `action` and `ipAddress` are declared in nested blocks while the existing try declares both directly, so the merge redeclares a name used in an enclosing scope. It got an OUTER try wrapping the existing try/catch whole, which satisfies the spec's intent and has a second merit: the existing catch keeps exactly its former reach, so no exception semantics changed, only where the flag is lowered. Written into the entry's remarks with the CS0136 reason so it does not read later as an inconsistency between twin pages. All eight scattered clears deleted after individual tracing; the order-sensitive one at old 442 confirmed - `onPremConfirmPending = true;` still precedes the lowering, so Continue still renders enabled. Guard proof, 6 mutations, all size-preserving at 679 lines, all bit, none tuned: M1 1F/138P, M2 1F/138P, M3 1F/138P, M4 2F/137P, M5 1F/138P, and **M6 - the guard moved between the pair - failed BOTH `ForbiddenGuardSitesCarryNoGuard` and `TheExactlyOneGuardSitsWhereItIsRegisteredAndNowhereElse`**, which is the c394120 harness slice paying off on the first page after it. All four matcher predictions held empirically rather than by inspection (accessRight vs onPremAccessRight and access vs accessRight both safe under WholeWord, isSet vs bulkIsSet safe, the argument-position ReadOf match working, and the finally's `onPremTarget = null;` correctly read as an assignment). ScannerFalsePositives confirmed empty by re-running the scanner AFTER conversion - StuckFlags 0, and the three ungated lines it reports are exactly the three registered exemptions. Verified: build Release 0 errors / 23 warnings, 2649 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped, format exit 0, git diff --check exit 0, ASCII lint passed. 396 cross-line string concatenations swept for the Edit trailing-space signature, 0 found. Module 1.1.0 -> 1.2.0, no base app bump. No reviewer round: the 2026-08-31 decision makes those CRITICAL-only with an owner go, and the coder-side guard proof closes this. Five pages remain. **Process note worth keeping: this page took three agent turns, two of which produced nothing because the agent spent them re-reading to rebuild context. What fixed it was dispatching a FRESH agent with the previous one's spec inlined verbatim - captures, line anchors, substitutions, the full registry entry and the matcher predictions - and an explicit instruction not to read the reference files at all. It finished in one turn.**
- 2026-09-18 click-gating tier 1 page 5 of 9, IntuneDevices (docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md): 1628 lines, one registry entry, zero new test methods, ClickGate 139 -> 164, suite 2649 -> 2674 (+25). **The WipeNameConfirmed probe bites.** Verified first: the clause is named at exactly one site, `wipeConfirmName` is read at exactly one site inside it, and ExecuteActionAsync re-checks granular authorization, ticket presence, ticket validity and the protected principal but never reads it. Only the busy clause moved; the precondition was OR-ed alongside and registered in AnnotatedControls.PreserveClauses. Probe M1b, size-preserving, deletes only that clause: 163/164, naming the page, line 402 and the clause verbatim. A second independent net already existed - `IntuneDevicesPageTests.IntuneDevices_WipeRequiresTypingTheDeviceNameAndRetireDoesNot` asserts the same substring - so it has two tripwires. One honest limit measured: on a WHOLESALE rewrite (M1) the assertion reports only the FIRST clause lost and stops, because xUnit fails on the first Assert; the wipe clause is still protected, just not named in that message. **The predicate was partial as Revision 1 said, and the missing flag was `detailLoading`** - and widening it closed a live defect rather than a theoretical one: the Search button sat outside the gate, and SearchAsync clears `devices`, `deviceOutcomes`, the open confirm bar and the expanded detail row, so **a search started while a wipe was queued discarded that wipe's on-screen verdict outright** - the row is gone from the new list so neither the acting spinner nor the outcome alert can render, while the audit row and admin email still say it happened. Reachable by click and by Enter. Name kept as `ActionsDisabled` rather than renamed to IsBusy, deliberately: the name is quoted by docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md (105, 156, 566) and .agents/state.md:298, neither of which this slice may edit, so a rename would leave two governance files describing a member that no longer exists. **Nine confirm-bar gates turned out to be DEAD.** ExecuteActionAsync sets `confirmDeviceId = null` in the same synchronous run as `actingDeviceId = deviceId`, above its first await, so the bar unrenders on the very render that would first have shown those controls greyed - `disabled="@(actingDeviceId != null)"` could never be true while they existed. Widened to the predicate, they are live for the one case that can happen: a detail read in flight with a confirm bar open. Cancel at 407 is dead the same way and was recorded rather than quietly changed, because the honest gate for a Cancel is no gate. Falsification 6 bit in an unexpected place: ExecuteActionAsync needed NO snapshot (it already binds eleven values above its first await and reads only locals afterwards), while **SearchAsync read `searchTerm` twice below `await Task.Yield()` on a box that binds on oninput** - so a keystroke landing in the yield made the IntuneDevices_Search audit record name a term Graph was never asked for, and that record is the page's only trace that someone's devices were looked up. Twelve excluded staged fields, not the predicted seven - censused by hand because Revision 1 gives a count and no list; the five wipe-option fields are probably what the prediction folded together, and the nastiest is `showActionHelp`, a page-level bool with no operation behind it, so folding it in would deaden every control for as long as the help panel stays open - the panel an operator opens BEFORE acting. One keyboard path, refused by the disabled attribute, with the handler's own `!isSearching` widened to `!ActionsDisabled` as belt and braces. **No live 2eb8c15 defect existed here** - button and input both read isSearching, so they agreed; the hazard was the CONVERSION, since gating the button on the widened predicate without the input would have manufactured one. Probe M7 confirms the pairing is enforced in both directions. Guard proof, 8 mutations, all bit, none tuned, all restores hash-clean. Verified: build Release 0 errors / 23 warnings, 2674 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped, format exit 0, git diff --check exit 0, ASCII lint passed, trailing-space sweep 0 hits. Post-conversion scanner: 9 buttons, Ungated 1 and GatePart 1 - exactly the two registered exemptions - Flags 3, Predicate ActionsDisabled, StuckFlags 0; unlike pages 3 and 4 the detector DOES see this page, because it has three flags rather than one. Module 1.3.0 -> 1.4.0, no base app bump. **Four things found and not fixed, all needing an owner go.** (1) The server-side wipe-name re-check. The agent recommends doing it and gave a concrete shape matching what ExecuteActionAsync already does; markup is one round trip stale by construction, so the typed-name clause is the only thing between an operator and a factory reset they did not type the name for. Behaviour change, out of sweep scope, and state.md already records it as needing a go. (2) Residual double-dispatch on Confirm: the browser's copy of the disabled attribute is one round trip stale and there is no calling method to guard, since ExecuteActionAsync IS the click target; the guard that would work also silently swallows a legitimate click landing in the stale window during a detail read, with no banner and no audit row, which is exactly the harm ForbiddenGuardSite exists to prevent - so ExactlyOneGuard is empty and the trade-off is left as a decision. (3) Doc drift this slice may not fix: plan 106 and 566 are now history, 520 says seven excluded fields against a hand census of twelve, and state.md:298 calls IntuneDevices incomplete when it no longer is. (4) **RiskyUsers.razor has the identical partial shape** - `ActionsDisabled => isLoading || actingUserId != null`, missing `historyLoading`, 4 of 7 buttons outside the gate. Tier 3, not approved, untouched; noted because the fix is now a known pattern.
- 2026-09-19 click-gating shared-component contract change, all three autocompletes (docs/ClickGatingAudit-Plan.md, owner decision 2026-09-18 which puts this in scope explicitly while forbidding it to become a licence to edit the pages it reaches). Split out of page 6 deliberately: it touches pages nobody approved, the mechanism is a real decision rather than a mechanical edit, and it is the first slice in the sweep where the BASE APP VERSION is in play. **Both mechanisms, not one, and the reasoning is the point.** Markup withholds the suggestion rows while Disabled (a new extracted `ShouldRenderSuggestions` predicate, extracted so a test can reach it, same justification as the existing ShouldShowTypeBadge), AND `SelectResult` refuses on entry as its literal first statement. Neither alone suffices: withholding is a network round trip away, so a mousedown dispatched before the diff lands still reaches the handler and loses that race; a handler guard alone leaves rows visibly clickable through the whole write, silently doing nothing, which trains operators to distrust the control. Rejected outright: `disabled` on an `<li>` (inert on a non-form element - the browser fires @onmousedown regardless, the exact looks-refused-acts-live shape falsification 2 warns about) and CSS pointer-events (would genuinely refuse but loses the same race while leaving a dead-looking dropdown). **Falsification 2's desync test was answered in the affirmative rather than assumed:** a handler guard is safe HERE because the suggestion rows derive from a directory/Get-Recipient search, not from the page's bound value, so refusing leaves the input rendering the same Value the server holds and the operator changed nothing in the DOM by clicking an li. That is the distinction that makes the guard legitimate on this control and illegitimate on a checkbox. In-flight search results survive - withholding is a pure render-time decision and clears neither _results nor _showDropdown. **The census was short by one: ADGroupAutocomplete is also affected AND is instantiated** (ModuleConfig:446, and that site DOES pass `Disabled="@saving"`, so the defect was live there) - 18 sites across 8 pages, not the 17 the brief carried. - 2026-09-21 queue item 8, the rebuild the first live run forced, plus its codex round. **The shipped module returned nothing at this tenant and the reason is worth keeping: `GET /api/machines` caps at 10,000 rows and emits NO `@odata.nextLink`.** R1(g) had listed the cursor as an open question with a `$skip` fallback that Revision 1 had already argued against; the live answer is that there is no cursor at all, so no paging strategy of any kind can list a 40,000-device tenant. The owner's ruling was blunt - "I will not approve any code that limits what this can do" and "anything that is obtainable via Microsoft's portal needs to be obtainable here" - and the refusal, which was correct by the module's own rules, was useless to them. **The design lesson: when the answer cannot be paged, divide the question.** The inventory is partitioned on `lastSeen` into disjoint, exhaustive ranges; a part under the cap has proved itself, a part AT the cap has proved nothing and is split and re-asked, and the answer is the union of the proved parts. No ordering assumption, no `$skip`, no cursor. Rows from an ambiguous parent are counted against the ceiling but deliberately **not** promoted - keeping them would still be correct and would quietly mask a partition whose children do not meet, because the boundary device would already be in hand. Null `lastSeen` satisfies neither `ge` nor `lt` and was the load-bearing case: covered by the undivided root, then by an explicit `lastSeen eq null` part the moment the root splits, and out of scope when the operator sets a window. Cost scales with the ratio of matching devices to the cap, not the device count - 4.3x measures at 12 requests. **Portal parity meant admitting what the API cannot do:** seven filters go server-side, four (machine tag, machine group, First seen, "any Windows") cannot be expressed against this collection, so they are applied after the fetch, labelled `(after fetch)` on the page, and named in the ceiling refusal as filters that will NOT reduce it - because sending an operator to narrow a filter that cannot shrink the fetch is a loop with no exit. **Codex round on the rebuild: `unsound`, 1 MEDIUM and 4 LOW, no CRITICAL and no HIGH.** It cleared the three things that mattered - completeness (siblings disjoint and exhaustive, nulls covered on every path, only Complete renders or exports), termination (split points strictly inside the range, fail-closed on an undividable part), and that client-side filters run AFTER the partition and never during it, since filtering while accumulating makes a full response of non-matching rows look short and turns ambiguity into a false proof of exhaustion. F1 (MEDIUM) was the owner's own no-editorialising ruling, applied to the refusals but missed in four strings and the results header; the range count came off the header as implementation detail. F2-F5 were an enrichment warning that fired with no rows to warn about, a refusal test asserting four of seven server-side filter names, **a budget test whose floor and ceiling were both 250 - it pinned the constant to itself while its name claimed headroom**, and an ordering guard covering one client-side filter of four. Both budget bounds are now derived (floor 10x a balanced division, ceiling a fifth of the documented hourly limit) and the ordering guard is a Theory over all four filters. Guard proof 4 probes, each attributable, M1 failing all four Theory cases; restores by copy + explicit `LastWriteTime` touch + SHA256 compare, never `git checkout --`.
