Nine implementation commits plus three review-fix commits; module `1.0.0`, base app `2.22.0` -> `2.23.0` for the `EmailService` method. Suite 2951 -> **3060 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped**. **The design reversal is the whole story: the destination is derived from the account's `employeeId` instead of typed by the operator.** The sixth revision had the operator typing an address, forced by a survey that found `employeeId` on 0 of 172 cloud accounts; the owner stamped them, so the reason evaporated and the security property came back with it - a typed address meant any operator could mail themselves any admin account's password and the plan had to admit its controls were detection rather than prevention. Deriving it removes the choice. Five refusals, none falling back: no id, no match, more than one match, no mailbox, lookup failed. The reveal permission overrides all five **including the unreachable directory**, which was nearly excluded until the owner challenged it - "a major incident where speed and access to creds is essential? and you want to fail closed, even for users who could normally see the password anyway?" - and both halves land: a reveal holder can already reach a password through the other four paths, so the gate stopped nobody determined while costing availability exactly when it mattered. **A gate the motivated can walk around and the legitimate cannot is an availability cost wearing a security label.** Force-change defaults ON, reversing the 2026-09-10 default, because the password now travels by email and forcing a change makes it a one-time handover rather than a standing credential in a mailbox. **Ten review findings, four HIGH, all admitted and fixed, records cpr-4 through cpr-13.** Every one of the five slices came back `unsound`. **Four of the ten were the same mistake in different places: an unanswered question read as a negative answer** - a missing `onPremisesSyncEnabled` read as cloud-only (would have admitted a synced account whose password is mastered on-prem), a failed role read rendered as "None active" (understating a possible Global Administrator on the panel an operator reads before committing), a lost PATCH response audited as "no change was made" when Graph may have applied it, and before them the deleted survey's confident 0%. That rule is now stated in the plan and pinned by tests. **Three findings were guards that existed and did not bite, which is the more uncomfortable pattern.** cpr-9: a test asserting the destination was re-derived at write time PASSED on code that re-derived it from the cached preflight target, because it checked that the call happened rather than what it read from - only the directory half was fresh and the `employeeId` could be minutes old. cpr-13: a test asserted "fewer than 100 violations in 500 draws" where AC14 states a guarantee, so it would have passed with the rule half-working. cpr-11: the audit-shape claim was invisible to source-text guards entirely; writing a test that reads the emitted JSON back then found that AC18 had an exception nobody had noticed - `LogModuleAction` writes `error` as `success ? null : errorDetail` and `JsonlLogService` drops nulls twice over, so that one key genuinely cannot be uniform and the plan now says so, with the test pinning it as the ONLY key allowed to vary. **A test that cannot fail for the reason it was written reports coverage it does not have.** Two mutation-probe honesty notes: one attempted probe is explicitly NOT counted because it did not compile, and removing the cpr-13 repair was probed against the 500-draw test FIRST and stayed green - twenty shuffles almost always succeed, so sampling cannot see a rare-path guarantee; the direct unit test is what bites, and it is also what caught my first repair being wrong on `[0,0,1,1,2,2]`. cpr-12 is the one I would have shipped: the word list was validated by a count floor alone, so 7,771 copies of `aaa` would pass and every entropy score would be a fiction - now checked for distinctness, the documented 3-9 lowercase-plus-hyphen shape, and an exact count pinned to the provenance record. **Three deleted-work decisions along the way.** The employeeId coverage survey was built, corrected three times by the owner (interactive Graph login instead of the connection module's `GraphConnect`; `Get-MgUser -All` enumerating the tenant; then the same bulk pattern left in the AD side after being fixed on the Graph side) and then deleted, because its premise is a directory census and the owner's rule is no bulk directory operations. **The rule was recorded too narrowly the first time** - "do not enumerate" - which is why I removed `-All` and then wrote a forest loop that does the same thing; it now reads that a directory operation is scoped to one named subject at the moment something is being done to it, and a loop over a population is a census however each query is written. The owner's own true-up files then answered more of item 10 than the tool would have: 111 set, 28 in a second tenant, 2 absent, 8 with no AD owner. **Process cost worth recording: I read four sections of a 949-line plan before starting to code and had to be stopped and sent back to read it properly.** The sections I skipped were the ones that say what the module IS. Everything after that read went faster, not slower.

- 2026-09-24 queue item 14 slice S1, the Migration open batch made addressable. One session, one commit, fresh context after a clear. **The slice description said "drive the open batch from a query parameter"; four things it did not say cost more than the parameter itself did.** (1) The page prerenders - `Components/Routes.razor` renders the router statically and each page declares `@rendermode InteractiveServer` - so the whole lifecycle runs once on the server with no circuit and again on a fresh instance, and an unguarded `OnParametersSetAsync` would have made the `Get-MigrationBatchUser` call twice on any pasted link and discarded the first result. (2) The same guard carries the authorization latch: `access-denied` is a `forceLoad` bounce, so the component finishes its lifecycle on the way out and would have read migration data for someone who had just been refused the page. That one is a real finding, not a tidy-up. (3) `LoadMigrationStatus` had to split, because a pasted `?batch=` link needs the catalogue loaded WITHOUT the collapse that a refresh performs - which gave the page a second path to a reloaded table and made `SelectionIsPrunedWhenTheTableReloads`, anchored on the wrapper, a guard that would pass while the new path pruned nothing. Re-anchored onto the shared half. (4) `ClickGateRegistry` pins the page's line count as a fingerprint (2025 -> 2127) and cites five handler coordinates in its rationale strings; the suite failed on exactly that and the correct response was to re-check all nineteen registered control lines against the file rather than bump the number. None had moved - every insertion is inside `@code` below the `PendingActionConfirm` fragment - which is itself the useful finding, and the five prose coordinates were re-pointed. **The design choice worth keeping: the URL is the source of truth and `expandedBatch` is its mirror, with exactly two writers** - `OpenBatch` outbound, `SyncOpenBatchFromUrl` inbound - because a second writer means the page and the address disagree and the address is the one that survives a reconnect. A test pins the writer set by walking every assignment to its enclosing method. **The honest limit: no test in this repo renders a Blazor component, so nothing here proves what a browser does.** The risk is named in the plan's Acceptance rather than waved at: every `NavigateTo` from inside this component is an enhanced navigation, and the documented behaviour is that the interactive component is preserved and simply receives new parameters - but if it is torn down instead, opening a batch resets the tab, the catalogue and the selection, and S1 needs a different mechanism. This is the app's first query-parameter route, so there is no precedent to read the answer off. Five new tests, all five proved to bite by reverting the behaviour and watching the named test fail, restored by rewriting the file rather than copying it back so the mtime advanced. - 2026-09-25 queue item 14, the S1 openreview and the one finding it produced. Verdict `acceptable with changes` from codex (`@azure-openai-eus2-global/gpt-5.5-dzs` @ xhigh, fallback) over `69980f1..f43fc11`; it endorsed the approach as what it would itself have built and required one change. **The finding is the argument for running the review at all: making the open batch addressable created a race that S1's own reasoning had not considered, because the new entry point is not on the page.** Open batch A, open B, press browser Back before B's Exchange call returns; both loads are in flight and if B's lands last it publishes over A, so the page shows A's name above B's mailboxes and the per-mailbox action buttons act on B. Every pre-S1 path into a batch-user load sat behind a control carrying `disabled="@IsBusy"` and two loads simply could not overlap - the browser's Back button is the one entry point a Blazor page cannot disable, and S1 handed it the keys. **Two implementation judgements inside the fix were the real work, not the counter.** (a) The guard went INSIDE `ReplaceBatchUsers` rather than at the two cited call sites: that is the single permitted row assignment, so all six load paths are covered by construction, and the four that are unreachable from the URL today are exactly the ones a later slice will wire to something that is. (b) The in-flight flag is keyed on the batch NAME, not the generation, and the tidier-looking version was wrong: on the collapse path the superseding act starts no successor load, so a generation-keyed clear would strand `loadingBatchUsers` for the life of the circuit - a stuck flag being the worse failure, and one this repo already has a test class for. A residual is stated rather than hidden (A -> B -> Back -> Forward un-busies the page a moment early; rows stay correct). **Guard proof, and one probe that is recorded as not counting.** Three tests, each mutated and watched to fail. The third is the one worth having: capturing the generation AFTER the await compiles, reads almost identically to the correct code, and compares the counter with itself so the guard can never fire. **My first attempt at that probe did not compile** - the capture moved inside the `try` while the `catch` still referenced it - so it proved nothing and is logged as NOPROOF rather than quietly redone; the rewritten version bit. **Process cost worth recording, and it is mine.** I asked the owner whether to fix a defect the review exists to surface - a ceremonial question the no-ceremonial-questions rule already answered - and then, when told it was pointless, treated that correction as authorisation and started editing code. Worse, the explanations I then gave invented a consultation step ("consulted", "checked", "read") that does not exist, since the invariant is in the same context that produces every token. The honest record is that the rule was fully present, I produced output that broke it, and dressing that up as a lookup failure was a second thing to stop doing. - 2026-09-25 queue item 14 slice S4, the outcome preview. Confirm carries the eligible count, every ticked row is annotated "Will run" or "Skipped (Status)" before the ticket is typed, the skipped list is named in full, and the picked action is filled while the other four stay outline. **The design question worth recording: a preview computed at staging time is stale by the time Confirm is pressed.** The operator can sit at the ticket field indefinitely while the table reloads underneath them - the same staleness class as pps-1(b), where an on-prem write used a protection verdict computed before a confirmation dialog. The resolution is that `pendingActionPlan` feeds the PREVIEW only and `StageSelectionAction` plans twice: once to show, once inside the callback at execution time. A test asserts both `Plan(` calls exist and bites when the second is replaced by the first, which is the exact edit a later refactor would make to "remove the duplicate". **Second decision: rows outside the staged plan say nothing.** Annotating an unticked row would claim an outcome for a row the action will never look at, which is a confident answer to a question nobody asked. **Third: the chip is text, not colour.** R17 refuses an unlabelled value, and the status that caused a skip travels with the word because a skip the operator cannot explain is one they cannot act on; the dimming is a redundant second signal, never the only one. **Process note, and it cost real time: `dotnet test` invoked through the Bash tool left orphaned `testhost` and `ExchangeAdminWeb.Tests` processes holding the test DLL,** so three consecutive runs failed with MSB3027 file-lock errors that look nothing like test failures and read as a broken build. The fix is to kill the orphans before each run and invoke the suite only through PowerShell; the probe script now does that itself. Guard proof: three new tests, each mutated and watched to fail - dropping the count from Confirm, collapsing the double plan into one, and reducing the skip chip to a bare "Skipped" with no status. One probe anchor needed rewriting because PowerShell ate the `$` in an interpolated C# string; recorded rather than silently retried. Module `1.12.0` -> `1.13.0`; `ClickGateRegistry` fingerprint 2433 -> 2528, the fourth re-derivation in four slices.

- 2026-09-30 queue item 17 slice S2 Graph I/O (Opus 5, one session, resumed from a handoff). `Services/CloudSignInService.cs` plus 21 tests; suite 3318 -> 3339. **The slice's real work was one architectural obstacle, and the cheap answer was wrong.** Non-interactive sign-ins do not exist on Graph v1.0 - the v1.0 `signIn` resource has no `signInEventTypes` and its list is documented as interactive-only - and the shared `GraphTokenClient` refuses any path outside `/v1.0` by a deliberate anti-exfiltration guard. Three ways out: widen the shared client (rejected - shared infrastructure used by eleven services, and adding a module must not bump the base app version), copy its token logic a third time (rejected - `DefenderApiClient` already exists because of the same constraint and agonises in its own doc comment about the duplication), or reuse `DefenderApiClient`, which takes base URL and scope as constructor arguments and is already built twice against two different APIs. The third, with the rationale written at the top of the new file so the next reader does not re-litigate it. **The measurement that a refactor would delete:** the source script deliberately uses the `/users` COLLECTION form with an `eq` filter rather than `/users/{id}?$select=signInActivity`, because the single-user form returned no activity for users who demonstrably had it. That is a fact about the tenant, not a style preference, so there is a wire-level test asserting the collection form and naming the reason. **The load-bearing rule: the log has ANSWERED only when both its queries did.** Relaxing that `&&` to `||` was probed and four tests fail - the account under test flips from "signInActivity alone, not evidence" to "confirmed dormant", which is the exact reading that gets a live account disabled. Two more probes: sending the non-interactive query to v1.0 fails five tests, and skipping an unreadable log date instead of discarding the source fails one. **One test caught my own wrong expectation rather than a code defect** - the guest-UPN escaping assertion expected `%23EXT%23` when the filter correctly lowercases to `%23ext%23`, which is what the sign-in log stores. A plan review is not a code review and saying so unprompted is the job. **All four findings were real, verified against the code before being accepted, and three were in the 30 lines I wrote fastest.** (1) A click is not a navigation: `UnsavedChangesGuard` can refuse one with `PreventNavigation`, and a refused navigation fires no load event, so cancelling that confirm left the PERMANENT frame reading "Loading Module Config - 94s" forever with an interval still repainting. The fix is exact rather than a timeout guess, which is only possible because Blazor pushes the new URL BEFORE it fetches and only after the location-changing handlers approve - so unchanged href plus no beforeunload plus no start event means the navigation never happened. (2) `OnInitializedAsync` awaits authentication before subscribing, so a circuit disposed during that await runs `Dispose` with `subscribed == false`, unsubscribes nothing, and then the continuation subscribes a dead component to a SINGLETON event where it stays for the process lifetime. (3) The handlers are `async void` and the refresh path reads SQLite, so a locked jobs database became an unhandled exception that tears down the operator's whole circuit - losing their page because a status bar could not repaint. (4) Dispose completed stragglers as failed but never cancelled their tokens, which is the orphaned-work defect this system exists to remove, reintroduced in the one place nobody looks; latent until the first module adopts, which is exactly why it was worth fixing before S4 spreads the assumption. **One finding per commit, four commits.** **The probe round caught a vacuous test of mine for the fourth time this week, and it was BOTH a bad probe and a weak test - the trap my own token-log entry warns about.** The guard asserted the watchdog's NAME appeared in the file; renaming the declaration and leaving the call sites satisfied it, because a guard that is present but never called reads identically to one that works. Rewritten to count call sites rather than mentions. Then the corrected probe reported a broken restore, which turned out to be a second bug in the same test: my regex counted call parentheses, and one of the two call sites passes the function to `setTimeout` BY NAME with no parentheses at all. Two defects in one assertion, both found by probing rather than by reading. **Counting references is not counting uses, and a name is not a wiring.** All four probes bite after the repair.

Every one of the three defects found in this module today - edl-1, edl-2, edl-3 - lived in presentation or reporting, which is precisely the surface the suite cannot see.
- 2026-10-05  queue item 18, edl-3's guard reopened and repaired: centralise, do not count (opus-5, same session). Suite steady at 3634. **The reviewer rejected my fix's TEST while accepting the fix itself, and that distinction is the whole lesson.** My guard counted `confirmed = false;` and the re-arm across the code block and asserted `reArmSites == clearingSites + 1`. It proves the one mutation I probed and not the invariant its own failure message claims: add an unrelated re-arm somewhere while a new clearing path omits one, and the arithmetic still balances. **A guard that is true of today's code is not the same as a guard that states the rule**, and the failure message I wrote described a rule the assertion did not enforce - which is worse than no message, because it tells the next agent the hole is covered. The repair took its first recommendation: `PerformLookup` and `ResetForm` both call one `BeginNewOperation()` helper, and `confirmed = false;` is pinned to exactly one site, so a third entry point cannot clear operation state without calling the helper or failing. **Centralising made the defect class structurally unreachable where counting only made it detectable.** Probed with the reviewer's OWN bypass - an inline `confirmed = false; resolvedPrincipal = null;` replacing the helper call - which fails both guards and which the counting version would have waved through. **Dispatched the repair delta at the SAME tier and said so in the prompt:** this machine's frontier pair is an alias of standard graded `fallback`, so a T5 bump buys nothing, and the playbook's answer for a second unsound verdict would have been the owner, not another round. Worth stating in the prompt rather than just the record - it tells the reviewer its verdict is terminal. **It reported two deviations instead of hiding them, both now in the finding:** the solution-level `dotnet test` hung in its shell for the mutation probes so it ran the same filters against the test project directly (the unmutated run WAS solution-level), and its safety wrapper blocked deleting its temp copy. **Three defects in one module in one session, and all three were presentation or reporting** - a red row, a hidden failure, a stale checkbox. None was reachable by any test this repo can write, because there is no bUnit harness. That is not an excuse for the three; it is the reason the per-slice review is load-bearing here rather than ceremonial, and the reason the manual acceptance list is the real gate.
- 2026-10-06  queue item 18, the owner reversed his own default: lockdown checkbox now UNCHECKED (opus-5, fresh day, same session). Module `1.3.0` -> `1.3.1`; base app unchanged; suite 3634 -> 3636. **A one-line owner instruction that contradicts a recorded ruling is not a contradiction to escalate - it is a supersession to record.** The 2026-10-05 ruling ("default checked... we need this working with an escape hatch") is still in `state.md` with its reasoning intact, now explicitly marked superseded and carrying a line telling the next reader NOT to reinstate default-checked from that reasoning alone. Deleting the old ruling would have been easier and would have lost why the design is shaped the way it is; leaving it unmarked would have let a future agent "fix" the default back. **The implementation lesson is that a value the owner has changed once will change again, so it stopped being a literal.** Three sites have to agree about the default - field initialiser, post-auth read, fresh-operation reset - and flipping two of three gives a session whose first operation behaves differently from the rest, which is edl-3's exact shape in a new direction. One `LockdownDefaultArmed` constant, and a guard asserting it appears at all three sites with no stray `moveToLockdownOu = true/false;` anywhere. **The config gate ANDs with the default rather than replacing it** (`LockdownDefaultArmed && lockdownOuConfigured`), so flipping the default back to armed still cannot arm a step with nowhere to move the account; probed. **The warning text had to flip with the default and this is edl-1 again.** "Without this, the account can be re-enabled by anyone with routine helpdesk rights" was red, shown when unticked - correct when unticking was a deliberate act, wrong the moment unticked became the default state, because a default must never be styled as an error. Now muted, and phrased as what ticking buys rather than as a reproach for a box the operator has not touched. **Noticing that was the only judgement call in the change; the owner asked for a default flip and said nothing about the warning.** **A perl `s{}{}` without /g replaced the FIRST match in a 1,100-line catalog, not the one I meant** - checked `git diff` rather than assuming, and it happened to be correct, but the habit that saved it was diffing before committing, not the regex.
- 2026-10-06  the startup Graph-secret remap was deleting Emergency Disable's on-prem credential reference on every boot; narrowed, tested, shipped (opus-5, same session). Base app `2.27.0` -> `2.27.1` (shared service, so the app version moves and no module version does); suite 3636 -> 3640. **The owner reported it as an environment problem - "why did it lose the delinea secret ID for on-prem? this seems to happen a lot between dev and prod" - and it was a code bug with a one-line cause.** `MigrateGraphSecretKeys` selected modules by "declares `GraphDelineaSecretId`" and treated any `DelineaSecretId` beside it as pre-rename residue; `RemapKey` then deletes the old row UNCONDITIONALLY, outside the copy branch. EmergencyDisable declares both as live fields, so every startup deleted its AD credential reference - and when the Graph slot happened to be empty, MOVED the AD secret ID into the cloud credential field, which is a credential-isolation problem rather than only a lost setting. **The "dev vs prod" framing was the misleading part and worth naming as a diagnostic pattern:** both instances share one config DB and the migration is unguarded at `Program.cs:366`, so a restart of EITHER instance erased a value the operator had just set on the other. Nothing was being promoted or copied; the symptom's shape pointed at deployment and the cause was startup. **The original defect was a reasoning error preserved in a comment.** `docs/GraphSecretKeyMigration-Plan.md:38` classified EmergencyDisable as "GraphDelineaSecretId only (no fallback)" - true of what its Graph CLIENT reads, false of what the module DECLARES - and the code comment then asserted "on-prem modules that legitimately use DelineaSecretId as their current key are never touched", which the predicate did not implement. **A comment claiming a property the code does not have is worse than no comment: it is what stops the next reader checking.** The existing `Migrate_DoesNotTouch_OnPremModules` test used ConferenceRooms, which declares no Graph key at all and therefore passed the original predicate trivially - coverage shaped like the claim instead of like the risk. **The owner said "no it is not" to my diagnosis and never said which part, and I did two things right and one thing wrong about that.** Right: I kept the dispute recorded in the plan rather than closing it, and I did not implement on a contested cause. Wrong: my first review dispatch handed codex the plan containing my diagnosis and I nearly reported its agreement as corroboration - it was not, it had read my conclusion. The honest version came from "do the review correctly": codex got the operator's symptom verbatim, a prohibition on reading the plan or any commit from the pinned head onward, and confirmed compliance unprompted. **It found the same mechanism from the symptom alone, rated HIGH, and cited two evidence lines I had missed** (`ModuleCredentialService.cs:27-31`, `EmergencyDisableService.cs:195-199` - where the AD path still reads the deleted key, which is what makes the deletion fatal rather than cosmetic). **An unprimed review is a different instrument from a primed one and I had been reaching for the wrong one.** **It also found a second config-loss mechanism I had not looked for**, now recorded as needing its own plan: whole-module save deletes every row for the module and rewrites from a snapshot the page loaded on open, so a stale tab erases rows written elsewhere. Also dev/prod-shaped. Not folded in - it fits "one value repeatedly" worse than the startup bug, and bundling it would have made the blast radius larger than the bug. **Effort `max` re-probed on codex-cli 0.159.0 and still rejected by this gateway; xhigh is the real ceiling, recorded so the next session does not read it as a shortcut.**

Test project only; no `.razor` touched, no version bumps; suite 3640 -> 3651. **The owner reported one page with a dead progress bar and the survey found 41, but the finding that mattered was that the test meant to prevent it could not see it.** `GlobalProgressWiringTests` checks each page for ONE `Progress.Begin` anywhere in the file, so `BlockedSenders` passed while its Exchange write was silent - and its page list is hand-maintained, so three pages that report nothing were never checked at all. A per-FILE check had been recorded as per-OPERATION adoption when queue item 24 closed. **My first design for the replacement would have been vacuous in exactly the same way and codex killed it before a line was written.** I had written that the guard could credit "this method, or something it directly calls"; `ConfirmUnblock` calls `LoadBlockedSenders`, which HAS an activity, so the reported defect would have passed the guard written to catch it. That is the fourth vacuous-guard near-miss in four days and the first one caught at DESIGN time rather than by a mutation probe - which is the cheapest place yet. **The fix was to make entries name the call they cover and assert source-order dominance**, with one explicit escape hatch (`DelegatesTo`) that requires the delegate to be registered in its own right. **Delegating the build to a subagent kept ~1,700 lines of registry out of this context entirely** and it came back with five independent probes, each isolating exactly one test - the stronger result, because it shows five conditions rather than one assertion wearing five names. **It also found 11 gaps the survey missed and refused to record them as Exempt**, on the grounds that doing so would bake in a false clean bill; and it checked two of its own suspicions that turned out NOT to be errors, which is the part that makes the other eleven credible. **Two operational notes for the next session:** a subagent died mid-run to a connection drop and resuming it by message preserved its whole reading of the plan and codebase - far cheaper than relaunching; and the codex review of S1 was killed by the harness for low system memory, leaving 312MB of disposable worktree copies under `.tmp/` that it could not clean up itself. S1 is therefore COMMITTED BUT UNREVIEWED.
- 2026-10-07  progress coverage S2, first fix: `CloudPasswordReset.ExecuteResetAsync` reports, and its destination derive leaves the renderer thread (opus-5). Module `1.0.3` -> `1.0.4`, base app unchanged at `2.27.1`; suite 3651 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped; registry 52 KnownGap -> 51. **The reporting half alone would have been a vacuous fix and the plan said so before a line was written.** `DeriveDestination` is `Get-ADForest` plus one `Get-ADUser` per forest domain, in series, behind a 30-second throttle, and it ran on the circuit's dispatcher - so the page could not repaint, and an activity wrapped around it would have satisfied all three `Reported` conditions while the operator watched exactly what they watch today. `LookupAsync` got this hop on 2026-10-02 and this path was named and deferred in the same entry; the deferral is what made it the first S2 fix rather than the fifth. **The claim the token log made about safety was verified rather than carried forward:** `ADEmployeeIdLookup.FindUserByEmployeeId` really does take `_runspaceLock.Wait(TimeSpan.FromSeconds(30))` before executing, so the pool hop cannot race a concurrent lookup. **Two source-text guards were pinned to the OLD inline form and had to move with the fix** - `CloudPasswordResetWritePathTests` asserted the literal `destination = ResetService.DeriveDestination(fresh);`, and a catalog test pinned `Version = "1.0.3"`. Neither was a design question, but both are the reason "one fix, four files" is normal here and a diff that touches only the page should be suspected of missing a pin. **Covered call named deliberately as the PATCH, not the derive.** Condition 3 asserts the first `activity.Complete(` comes after the named call, so naming the LAST heavy call also pins the single `Complete` into the `finally`: an early `Complete` added to any refusal path later would land before the PATCH and fail. Naming the derive would have proved less. **Both halves probed and both bite:** stripping the `Begin`/`Step`/`Complete` calls failed exactly one test, `AReportedOperationOpensItsActivityBeforeTheCallItCovers`, naming `CloudPasswordReset.razor: ExecuteResetAsync` (1 failed / 10 passed); reverting the `Task.Run` failed exactly one test, `The_destination_is_re_derived_at_write_time_not_taken_from_preflight` (1 failed / 21 passed). Restored by `cp` plus an explicit `touch`, per the mtime trap this repo has been burned by. **The page is NOT ClickGate line-pinned** - it sits in the registry's declared-but-unconverted list with a reason string, so adding 33 lines invalidated nothing and no re-anchor pass was needed. That is why this was the cheap place to start a slice whose other twelve pages each cost one.

The filter is what made five probes affordable; the full suite was run once per commit and no more. **The brief's two line-number claims were both verifiable in one `grep -n` and both held**, which is the cheapest possible check and saved a re-anchor round. **ClickGate re-anchoring cost nothing here because the insertion was in the `@code` block**: every line-keyed entry on `ConferenceRooms` sits at or below 575 and the guard went in at 735, so a single `git diff -U0` hunk header (`@@ -734,0 +735,10 @@`) proved the whole map and only `ExpectedLineCount` moved. Nothing asserted the ~30-second derive was inside the activity, so hoisting it above the `Begin` would have passed every test while reinstating exactly the behaviour the fix removed. **The proof is the cleanest in this log: the same mutation FAILS under the new multi-call entry and PASSES 12/12 under the old one - same page, same edit, only the registry entry differs.** **The fix generalised rather than special-cased:** `Reported` keeps its single-call constructor so ~50 entries are untouched, and gains a list form where `Begin` must dominate EVERY named call and the activity must still be open at each. `TrueLastLogon.SearchAsync` had the same defect and its note already admitted two halves while holding one. **Three hardenings came with it, and one of them closes a hole the multi-call change CREATED:** an entry naming nothing would have passed conditions 1-3 vacuously by having nothing to iterate, so an anti-vacuity test now requires at least one name. **Widening a guard can open a vacuity hole in the same stroke - check for that every time.** **The `@onchange` sweep is the part I trust least and said so when dispatching the review:** 25 handlers surfaced, all 25 classified Exempt, zero new KnownGap. That is either careful work or exemption-washing to reach green, and the agent's defence is that it read every body AND every helper they call, with the two Migration ones held by `DelegatesTo` rather than a bare exemption so breaking the chain fails a test. Codex was asked to spot-check at least six across different pages. **The agent broke its own work, which is what I asked for and had not got before:** it found a REAL bypass - a string literal containing the covered-call text satisfied condition 1, observed passing 12/12 - and closed it by blanking literals when locating calls but not when discovering handlers, because markup attributes are quoted. **It then recorded five bypasses it could NOT close**, including a method group handed to a dispatcher, which it argues is not closable with regex at all. A list of attempted bypasses with their outcomes is worth more than a green suite, and this is the first slice in this log to produce one unprompted. **Also worth recording: `dotnet test` on the solution did NOT stall for this agent across four full cycles**, while two consecutive codex reviews reported it hanging. - 2026-10-07  prog-3 fixed: the progress guard's blanker now tells code from data, and the record says which scan reads which view (opus-5, subagent). Test project only, no version bump; suite 3661 -> 3674. **The finding's own words were the brief: "closing one literal hole and leaving its twin open is a worse outcome than not having looked."** prog-2 blanked literals when LOCATING a covered call and not when FINDING the activity, so a method containing the text of a `Progress.Begin` had one as far as the regex was concerned. Both probes passed 12/12 before the fix and fail after - same probe, only the scanner differs. **The asymmetry that caused it is real and had to be KEPT, which is why the fix is a second view rather than more blanking.** Handler names are quoted markup attribute values, so discovery must read literals intact; everything that asks what a method DOES reads a code view with comments and literals blanked. The record carries a scan-to-view table, because the previous agent held that distinction in its head and shipped half of it. **The blanker is now one lexical pass over comments AND literals, not two, and that was not in the brief.** Comment-first ends a line at any `//`, including one inside a string, which deletes that string's closing quote; a VERBATIM literal then runs past its line and the next literal pairs one quote out of step, leaving string content readable as code. **I nearly shipped a test row for that which could not fail** - the single-line form is self-limiting, because a non-verbatim literal ends at the newline and a comment-first pass swallows the payload with the rest of the line. Caught by mutating the fix and watching the row stay green; rewritten with a verbatim literal, which bites. The non-discriminating row is kept with "this does NOT discriminate" written on it rather than deleted, so the obvious shape to try does not look untried. **Four mutations, each applied alone: prefix detection, the raw-literal arm, the two-pass change, and discovery moved to the code view.** The last one failed exactly ONE test - the new asymmetry guard - and left everything else green, which is precisely the vacuity it exists to catch and the reason it is worth its lines. **Eight bypass rows closed and five recorded as surviving**, the top one new: a `#if NEVER` region still reads as code to a lexical scanner. That and `if (false)` are the control-flow half, and both say Roslyn - which stayed untaken, because it is a live owner decision on `leak-1` and implementing it here would have settled it by stealth. **`perl -0pi` mangled a multi-line regex substitution again (third time in this log); the Edit tool did it first try.** Also: `python` is not on PATH on this host - `perl` is.

A handler with neither admits the strong mutation. **Counting drift, flagged not fixed:** `grep -cE 'new\("[A-Za-z0-9_]+", *[0-9]+'` over `ProgressRegistry.cs` gives 48 -> 47 KnownGaps for this slice and 49 -> 48 for `94361fd`, whose record says 50 -> 49.

**2026-10-07, `docs/ProgressCoverage-Plan.md` S2 sixth and LAST fix - `ExchangeOnlineConfig.SaveExoConfig` reports its save and its pool drain. S2 is complete; all six silent writes report.** `ExchangeOnline` `1.0.2` -> `1.0.3`; base app unmoved at `2.27.1`. Suite 3674, unchanged. **The page injected `IActivityProgress` nowhere and no test looked at it** - absent from `GlobalProgressWiringTests`' hand-maintained list, which is exactly the second hole the registry's filesystem-derived page set closes. **Answered the brief's measurement question: the drain IS slow, conditionally, and the survey never measured it.** `DrainPoolCore` empties the bag one runspace at a time, `DestroyRunspace` runs a synchronous `Disconnect-ExchangeOnline` per runspace, then `DrainPool` re-reads three connection values from the shared config DB uncached - so the cost is exactly the pool depth, zero to five EXO round trips against a five-slot cap, unknowable at the call site. Hence `ActivitySize.Unknown`: `Steps(2)` would claim the local SQLite write is half the wait. **Two calls named:** `ModuleConfigSvc.SaveModuleConfig(` as the opening pin because it is the MUTATION rather than the slow part, `ExoPool.DrainPool(` as the closing pin and the only slow call. Auth state, `AuthorizeAsync`, `GetModuleConfig` and `Audit.LogSettingsChange` read and not named. **The `Begin` went ABOVE this page's `await Task.Yield()`, which no earlier S2 fix needed:** everything after it here is synchronous, so the Yield is the only guaranteed flush point and an activity opened after it would pass the guard while the operator saw nothing. **Left open deliberately:** the drain still runs on the renderer thread. The bar paints before the freeze and stays painted through it, so the operator is told; `Task.Run` is a threading change the plan authorised only for `CloudPasswordReset`. **The hoist probe was STRONG again, by the other route** - the covered call `ExoPool.DrainPool();` moved above the method-scope `Begin`, no added lines, no preamble dragged along. Moving the `Begin` itself is still blocked where the `Complete` lives in a `finally`; hoisting the OPENING pin would have meant dragging its preamble, the weak substitution - hoisting the CLOSING pin needs neither, which is the general escape the last three slices missed.

**2026-10-07, `docs/ProgressCoverage-Plan.md` S3 THIRD fix - `MailboxPermissions.SubmitSingle` now opens its activity above the ticket validation and the protected-target check.** `MailboxPermissions` `1.2.1` -> `1.2.2`; base app unmoved at `2.27.1`. **2026-10-07, review finding `prog-6` - three page-test anchors move from raw text to the code view.** Test project only; no page, no registry, no version bump, suite 3674 unchanged. `IntuneDevicesPageTests` (two sites) and `RiskyUsersPageTests` (one) took `body.LastIndexOf("finally")` over RAW method text, so a comment containing that word below the admin send failed them against correct code - tripped for real by the `3c2df38` agent, reproduced by codex. **Fixed by reusing `ProgressScan.CodeView`, not `ClickGateSource.BlankComments`, and the reason is specific:** `BlankComments` refuses to blank a `//` comment on any line where a quote opens earlier, which is exactly the shape here (`Logger.LogError(ex, "..."); // finally ...` inside the catch that wraps every one of these sends), and `CodeView` additionally blanks string literals, the `prog-3` counterfeit class on the same scan surface. **What made it a small change is offset preservation** - pinned by `TheCodeViewIsTheSameLengthAndShapeAsTheSource` - so the anchor is found in the code view and the raw body is still sliced at that index, leaving the three assertions whose needle IS a string literal untouched. The `try` anchor and the two-send count moved to the code view with it; the count would otherwise be inflated by a comment naming the send. `ProgressRegistryTests.cs` referenced, not modified. **Guard proof is the mutation the finding predicts**, a "finally" inserted into an existing comment line below the last send on BOTH pages, line count unchanged: fixed tests 176/176 PASS, the same pages against the tests at `dbcb116` 3/176 FAIL with the predicted messages. Scored on the two-class filter, not the full suite.

**2026-10-08, `docs/ProgressCoverage-Plan.md` S4 SECOND AND LAST fix - `AdminBulkJobs`. S4 IS COMPLETE, and it was TWO pages rather than the plan's three: `ExchangeOnlineConfig` was closed by S2.** `AdminBulkJobs` `1.0.2` -> `1.0.3`; base app unmoved at `2.27.1`. Named under the reviewer's rule 1, operation-scale LOCAL work. **Two findings beyond the brief.** First, BOTH handlers were synchronous, so a wrapped activity would have opened and completed inside one stretch of renderer time and painted nothing; both are now `async Task` with `await Task.Yield()` between the `Begin` and the work, the `ExchangeOnlineConfig.SaveExoConfig` shape, and on `ToggleDetails` the Begin sits ABOVE the `detailsJobId` assignment so the intermediate frame does not show the new job's panel holding the old job's rows. Second, and it changed the design: `BulkJobService` raises `JobChanged` once per ROW while a job runs and `OnJobChanged` called `RefreshJobs`, so an activity there would strobe the frame ten thousand times during a ten-thousand-row job - while `GlobalProgress` already renders that job through `BulkJobService`'s own channel. The three store reads were extracted into a silent `LoadJobsFromStore` for that path; `RefreshJobs` keeps the name and the entry and is the operator-initiated reload only, with `CancelJob`/`RemoveJob` becoming `async Task` to await it, which keeps their existing exemption reason true. `RefreshJobs` therefore names its covered call THROUGH the helper, the `IntuneDevices`/`PerformActionAsync` indirection. **Neither Complete reads a field** - literal messages, so the post-await live-read question does not arise. **No re-anchor** (ClickGate tier 4, unconverted; both line pointers are the gaps this deletes). **Six probes, each ALONE, each on the FULL suite, every one 1 failed / 3673 passed single-fault**, both hoists the clean strong form with the covered call itself relocated above the `Begin` and the line count unchanged at 404.

Seven gaps close into THREE activities - `LoadEvents`, `LoadUsage`, `DownloadCsv` - with the four entry points above the two loaders registered `Exempt` + `DelegatesTo`. **All three handlers were fully SYNCHRONOUS** (`DownloadCsv`'s only await was the interop transfer, after the whole file had been built), so all three carry `await Task.Yield()`, and both sizes are `Unknown` rather than `Steps(3)`/`Items(n)` for the same reason: nothing between the `Begin` and the `Complete` can repaint. `LoadEvents` is markup-wired via `@bind:after` on the diagnostics toggle, names three local disk calls under rule 1, and carries three `Complete`s because its three exits differ - an empty range completes TRUE, an unreadable file FALSE, a throw falls to disposal. **`ExecuteUndo` changed, forced rather than chosen:** its trailing refresh now reports for itself, so its `Complete` moved above it, `BlockedSenders.ConfirmUnblock`'s shape; the one behaviour change is an improvement, a re-scan fault can no longer brand a committed reversal failed. **`OnDateRangeChanged` is half-pinned and says so** - `DelegatesTo` holds one name, so a two-branch dispatcher cannot have both branches held. **An existing guard tripped and was re-anchored, not weakened:** `UsageTrackerWiringTests` pins two handler signatures verbatim and both became `async Task`; only the two anchor strings changed, every assertion still bites. **No re-anchor owed** (ClickGate tier 4, unconverted). **Four probes, each ALONE, each on the FULL suite, every one 1 failed / 3673 passed single-fault** - strip and `Begin`-relocated hoist on `LoadEvents`, early `Complete` on `DownloadCsv` (the first of the seven silent CSV exports to be fixed), delegation probe on `OnDateRangeChanged`.

Both `Complete`s read the LOCAL `parsed`, not the preview field, because this page's own `ClickGateRegistry` entry records that a second file can be chosen before the `InputFile`'s disabled attribute reaches the browser. **Re-anchor owed and paid:** `ExpectedLineCount` `1647` -> `1686`; the highest line key on this page is 575 and both edits are above line 1040. **Three probes, each ALONE, each on the FULL 3677 suite, every one 1 failed / 3673 passed single-fault**, all line-neutral or commented out: strip of `HandleFinderCsvUpload` (condition 2, all three calls); its `Begin` relocated below the parse block, which is the form the brief prescribes when the covered call would drag a declaration - `ParseFinderCsvAsync(` is inside an `await using` (condition 2 on that call alone); early `Complete` above the loop on `HandleTypeCsvUpload` (condition 3, naming `GetRoomInfoAsync(`). **CORRECTION TO `430e929`:** its message and token-log line claim all nine of the CSV-export shape are closed. **2026-10-08, review finding `prog-9` AND THE SWEEP IT IMPLIES - one finding, one commit, three handlers fixed.** `MessageTrace` `1.5.6` -> `1.5.7` and `Migration` `1.22.7` -> `1.22.8`; base app unmoved at `2.27.1`. Suite 3674, unchanged. The reported case is `MessageTrace.ExportCsv`: it never raises `isLoading`, so Run Trace stays live, and `RunTrace` nulls `response` - the `Task.Yield()` this work stream added turned a guarded read into an unguarded one. It now captures `rows`, `rangeStart` and `rangeEnd` above the `Begin`, the dates included because the filename is built from them and their inputs are `isLoading`-gated too. **THE SWEEP IS THE FINDING'S LARGER HALF: eleven yields this stream added, eight SAFE, THREE FIXED.** Two of the three are `Migration`, and `430e929` cleared both on a premise that does not hold - "`isDownloadingCsv` is in `IsBusy` and is raised above the yield, so the page is shut". `ClickGateRegistry.Migration` itself records three deliberately UNGATED controls that write exactly the fields these handlers read after the yield: `ExemptControl(1210)` Close, which `DismissReportModal` uses to null `reportUser`/`userReport`/`reportFetchedAtUtc` - a NULL DEREFERENCE in `ExportOpenReportAsync`, the same class as the reported case, not a milder one - and `UngatedDomSyncedControl(959)`/`(1235)`, the mailbox tick boxes under owner ruling D2(a), which would have made `DownloadReportExportAsync` zip a set its own label had already counted differently. Capturing at the top of the handler does not contradict D2(a): the capture IS the live selection at click time. `Migration.DownloadCsvAsync` is the one handler cleared WITHOUT a capture and the clearance was checked rather than inherited - filter box `:518`, sort select `:529`, direction button `:539` all carry `disabled="@IsBusy"` and `migrationBatches` is written only by `LoadBatchList`. **A RUNTIME probe, which is unusual here and deliberate:** every other guard in this stream is a source scan, which is what could not have caught this. The real `ExportCsv` was driven by reflection on a dedicated thread under a `SynchronizationContext` whose first `Post` - the yield continuation - nulls `response` first, which is what the Blazor dispatcher does with a queued click. Before: `NullReferenceException ... MessageTrace.razor:line 971`, the exact line the finding named. After: passes, and the decoded CSV carries the snapshot's row. The probe file is NOT committed; adding a permanent reflection-plus-pump harness to a repo with no bUnit is an owner call. **Re-anchor owed and paid:** `Migration` `ExpectedLineCount` `4149` -> `4170`; first changed line is 2754 and the page's highest line key is 1498, checked against the sorted list. `MessageTrace` is ClickGate tier 4 with no line keys. Four `ProgressRegistry` notes corrected, three of which claimed no snapshot was needed.

An empty result completes TRUE (the reviewer's 2026-10-08 ruling, the `AdminEventLog` empty-range half); a Graph fault falls to the dispose fallback, because the `catch`'s banner is the refusal and the work did not finish. **No snapshot, and it was checked rather than waved off:** `upn` is hoisted above the `Begin` for the LABEL, and the UPN box at `MfaReset.razor:50` is `disabled="@isLoading"` with the raise above the pre-existing yield, so the field is shut for the whole window; the `catch`'s second `userPrincipalName.Trim()` now reads the same local. **2026-10-08, `docs/ProgressCoverage-Plan.md` S5 FOURTEENTH fix - `CalendarPermissions.DownloadCsvReport`, the first of the two the CSV-export shape was wrongly declared closed without.** `CalendarPermissions` `1.2.2` -> `1.2.3`; base app unmoved at `2.27.1`. Suite 3674, unchanged. The settled two-pin form without argument: `GenerateCsvReport(` opens, `JS.InvokeVoidAsync(` closes, `Complete` below the transfer, `ActivitySize.Unknown` with the count in the LABEL, `await Task.Yield()` because the projection, the per-FIELD sanitiser in `Models/BulkOperationResult.cs` and both encodes are synchronous and the only await was the transfer after them. **THE SNAPSHOT IS FORCED TWICE OVER HERE:** this handler never raises `isLoading`, so `IsBusy` is false for the whole export, AND the result banner's dismiss at `CalendarPermissions.razor:211` is literally `@onclick="() => bulkResult = null"` with no disabled attribute at all. Before this activity there was no suspension point between the guard and the projection; the yield creates one, and a queued dismiss would make the export throw - review finding `prog-9` written out in advance rather than found by a reviewer afterwards. **Re-anchor owed and paid:** `ExpectedLineCount` `713` -> `754`; the highest line key on this page is 211 (the dismiss button) and the only edit is at 692 inside the `@code` block, checked against the sorted list rather than assumed. No test pinned this module's version string. **Three probes, each ALONE, each on the FULL 3677 suite, every one 1 failed / 3673 passed single-fault**, all line-neutral or commented out so the page held at 754 lines: strip (condition 2, both calls, offsets 385 and 613); STRONG hoist with the covered projection itself relocated above the `Begin` (condition 2 on `GenerateCsvReport(` ALONE at offset 153 - `JS.InvokeVoidAsync(` stayed dominated, the proof the two-pin entry holds two ends); early `Complete` above the transfer (condition 3, offset 595 before 688). **ONE PROBE HAD TO BE RE-RUN AND THE TRAP IS WORTH RECORDING:** the hoist was first anchored on `using var activity = Progress.Begin(` alone, which occurs FOUR times on this page; perl replaces the first, the edit landed in `SubmitSingle` where `report` does not exist, the build failed and the run produced no assertion at all. **A probe that does not compile is not a passing probe and not a failing one - it is no evidence**, and it is silent, so check every probe actually produced a result line. Re-anchored on `var report = bulkResult;` and re-run. Page restored byte-identical by md5 (`6a3aa6d7...`) and touched after each.

**2026-10-08, `docs/ProgressCoverage-Plan.md` S5 SIXTEENTH fix - `ConferenceRooms`' bulk-jobs panel, two gaps in one commit, and THE LAST OF THE PLAN.** `ConferenceRooms` `2.6.6` -> `2.6.7`; base app unmoved at `2.27.1`. Suite 3674, unchanged. **`ProgressRegistry` now carries ZERO `KnownGaps`.** `ToggleJobDetails` is `AdminBulkJobs.ToggleDetails`' twin down to the unbounded `BulkJobs.GetRows(` - honest reporting of a problem that should not exist, with the missing LIMIT named in place as `docs/BoundedJobQueries-Plan.md`, unapproved and NOT fixed. **`RefreshJobs` could not simply be wrapped and the event-storm check was RE-RUN here rather than inherited:** `RaiseChanged` at `Services/Jobs/BulkJobService.cs:473` sits inside the per-row `RecordRow` loop, so `JobChanged` fires once per ROW, and `OnJobChanged` called `RefreshJobs` - an activity there would strobe the frame ten thousand times during a ten-thousand-row job while `GlobalProgress` already renders that job for its submitter through `BulkJobService`'s own channel. The three store reads were split into a deliberately silent `LoadJobsFromStore`, which `OnJobChanged` now calls; `RefreshJobs` keeps the reporting and is the operator-initiated reload only, naming its covered call THROUGH the helper because condition 1 needs the fragment in the method's own code. `RemoveJob` became `async Task` to await it; the markup lambda at `:575` is unchanged. Both handlers were fully synchronous and both now carry `await Task.Yield()`. No snapshot on either, checked against `prog-9`: everything below `ToggleJobDetails`' yield derives from the `jobId` PARAMETER and its fields are written not read back - which is also the condition `ClickGateRegistry`'s exemptions at 508 and 572 rest on. **ONE CLICKGATE ENTRY WAS SPLIT TO FOLLOW THE CODE:** `ForbiddenGuardSites` had one `RefreshJobs` entry whose reason had two halves, and the background half is now false of `RefreshJobs` and true of `LoadJobsFromStore`; each half went with its method rather than leaving a registry reason that does not describe the code. **Re-anchor owed and paid:** `ExpectedLineCount` `1686` -> `1758`; highest line key 575, first changed line 748. `EventSubscriptionLifetimeTests` stayed green - the `_disposed` guard still precedes the `+=` and the new await sits below both. No test pinned this module's version string. **Three probes, each ALONE, each on the FULL 3677 suite, every one 1 failed / 3673 passed single-fault**, all commented out so the page held at 1758 lines: strip of `ToggleJobDetails` (condition 2, `BulkJobs.GetRows(`); strip of `RefreshJobs` (condition 2, `LoadJobsFromStore(` - the structurally distinct one, the probe that proves the HELPER indirection is actually pinned); early `Complete` above the unbounded read (condition 3). Page restored byte-identical by md5 and touched after each.

**2026-10-08, review finding `prog-7` - a ClickGate exemption's written condition was false.** `MailboxPermissions` and `CalendarPermissions` both `1.2.3` -> `1.2.4`; base app unmoved at `2.27.1`. Suite 3674, unchanged. The `:179`/`:178` dismiss exemption said "SubmitSingle and ExecuteOnPrem both hold their outcome in a local opResult and only ever assign to result", and both `ExecuteOnPrem` `finally`s read the field back - `onPremActivity.Complete(result?.Success ?? false, result?.Message)`. **The finding offered a reword or a code change and the code change was taken, argued in the record rather than asserted:** the two candidate conditions are not the same size, "no handler reads `result` back" is one grep and "no `await` sits between the last write and the `finally`'s read" is a two-clause argument re-derived over 110 lines on two pages; and the read was INTRODUCED by this stream's own global-progress sweep into a `finally` that previously read nothing, which is evidence the invariant erodes under ordinary work. `PermissionResult? outcome` declared before the `try`, all four writes become `result = outcome = ...`, the `finally` completes from the local. **No guard to mutate and that is a property of the finding:** `ConditionThatKeepsItTrue` is prose by design (`ClickGateTests.cs:1576`). The proof is the re-check the wording is meant to make cheap - every whole-word `result` in both `@code` blocks enumerated, 25 and 26 hits, all declaration/assignment/prose, zero reads. **Re-anchor owed and paid:** `ExpectedLineCount` `800` -> `808` and `754` -> `762`, fired for real by `TheRegisteredLineCountStillMatchesTheFile`; highest line key 212 and 211, first changed line 670 and 624, both inside `@code`, checked against the sorted key list rather than assumed.
